Several terms redirect here. For other uses, see [John Paul II (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_II_\(disambiguation\)), [Pope John Paul II (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II_\(disambiguation\)), [Saint John Paul II (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_John_Paul_II_\(disambiguation\)), [JP2 (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JP2_\(disambiguation\)), and [Karol Wojtyla (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol_Wojtyla_\(disambiguation\)).

| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:John_Paul_II_series)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:John_Paul_II_series?action=edit\&redlink=1)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:John_Paul_II_series) |

**Pope John Paul II**[^cite_note-8] (born **Karol Józef Wojtyła**;[^cite_note-9] 18 May 1920 – 2 April 2005) was the head of the [Catholic Church](606848.md) and sovereign of [Vatican City](32408.md) from 16 October 1978 until [his death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_funeral_of_Pope_John_Paul_II) in 2005. He was the first non-Italian pope since [Adrian VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_VI) in the 16th century, as well as the [third longest-serving pope](23056.md#Longest-reigning_popes) in history, after [St. Peter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter) and [Pius IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pius_IX).[^cite_note-13] In addition to this, he was an important philosopher and theologian of the 20th century.

In his youth, Wojtyła worked occasionally as a stage actor. He graduated from an [all-boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-boys_school) high school in [Wadowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadowice), Poland in 1938, shortly before [World War II](32927.md) broke out. During the war, to avoid being kidnapped and sent to a [German forced labour camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_labour_under_German_rule_during_World_War_II), he signed up for work in harsh conditions in a [quarry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quarry). Wojtyła eventually took up acting and developed a love for the profession and participated at a local theatre. The linguistically skilled Wojtyła wanted to study [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language) in university. During his studies, he was encouraged by a conversation with [Adam Stefan Sapieha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Stefan_Sapieha) to study theology and become a priest. Eventually, Wojtyła rose to the position of [Archbishop of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Krak%C3%B3w) and then to [cardinal](6221.md), both positions held by his mentor. Wojtyła was elected pope on the third day of the [October 1978 conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_1978_conclave), becoming one of the youngest popes in over a century. The conclave was called after the death of [John Paul I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_I), who served only 33 days as pope. Wojtyła adopted the name of his predecessor in tribute to him.[^cite_note-14]

Pope John Paul II attempted to improve the Catholic Church's relations with [Judaism](15624.md), [Islam](6037917.md), and the [Eastern Orthodox Church](10186.md) in the spirit of [ecumenism](146656.md), holding [atheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) as the greatest threat. He maintained the Church's previous positions on such matters as abortion, [artificial contraception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_contraception), the [ordination of women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordination_of_women), and a celibate clergy, and although he supported the reforms of the [Second Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council), he was seen as generally conventional in their interpretation.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16] He put emphasis on family and identity, while questioning consumerism, hedonism and the pursuit of wealth. He was one of the most-travelled world leaders in history, visiting 129 countries during his [pontificate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontificate). As part of his special emphasis on the [universal call to holiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_call_to_holiness), Pope John Paul II [beatified 1,344 people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_beatified_by_Pope_John_Paul_II),[^cite_note-17] and [canonised 483 saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_saints_canonized_by_Pope_John_Paul_II), more than the combined tally of his predecessors during the preceding five centuries. By the time of his death, he had named most of the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals), consecrated or co-consecrated many of the world's bishops, and ordained many priests.[^cite_note-18] Pope John Paul II died on 2 April 2005, and was succeeded by [Pope Benedict XVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedict_XVI).

Pope John Paul II has been credited with fighting against [dictatorships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship) and with helping to end [communist rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_rule) in his native Poland and the rest of Europe.[^cite_note-19] Under John Paul II, the Catholic Church greatly expanded its influence in Africa and Latin America and retained its influence in Europe and the rest of the world. He was proclaimed [venerable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venerable) by Pope Benedict XVI on 19 December 2009, and on 1 May 2011 ([Divine Mercy Sunday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_Mercy_Sunday)) he was [beatified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatified). On 27 April 2014, [Pope John Paul II was canonised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canonization_of_Pope_John_XXIII_and_Pope_John_Paul_II) by [Pope Francis](1687680.md), alongside [Pope John XXIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_XXIII).[^cite_note-BBC_2013-20] [He has been criticised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_Pope_John_Paul_II) for allegedly not acting against or having been insufficiently harsh against the sexual abuse of children by priests, both as archbishop in the [Polish People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_People's_Republic)[^cite_note-21] and during his papacy.[^cite_note-22] After his canonisation, he has been referred to by some Catholics as "Pope Saint John Paul the Great", though that title is not official.[^cite_note-23]

Under Pope John Paul II, two of the most important documents of the contemporary Catholic Church were drafted and promulgated: the [1983 Code of Canon Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Code_of_Canon_Law), which revised and updated the [1917 Code of Canon Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1917_Code_of_Canon_Law), and the *[Catechism of the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_the_Catholic_Church)*, the first universal catechism to be issued since the [Roman Catechism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catechism).

## Early life

Main article: [Early life of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_life_of_Pope_John_Paul_II)



The wedding portrait of John Paul II's parents, Emilia and Karol Wojtyła Sr.

Karol Józef Wojtyła was born in the Polish town of [Wadowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadowice).[^cite_note-A\&E-24][^cite_note-ShortBio-25] He was the youngest of three children born to [Karol Wojtyła](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol_Wojty%C5%82a_\(senior\)) (1879–1941), an [ethnic Pole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_Pole), and [Emilia Kaczorowska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilia_Kaczorowska) (1884–1929), who was of distant Lithuanian heritage.[^cite_note-CNN6-26] Emilia, who was a schoolteacher, died from a heart attack and kidney failure in 1929[^cite_note-CBN-27] when Wojtyła was eight years old.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200611-28] His elder sister Olga had died before his birth, but he was close to his brother [Edmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Wojty%C5%82a), nicknamed Mundek, who was 13 years his senior. Edmund's work as a physician eventually led to his death from [scarlet fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scarlet_fever), a loss that affected Wojtyła deeply.[^cite_note-CNN6-26][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200611-28]



Wojtyła on the day of his [first communion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_communion)

Wojtyła was [baptized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacraments_of_the_Catholic_Church#Baptism) a month after his birth, made his [First Communion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Communion) at the age of 9, and was [confirmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmation_in_the_Catholic_Church) at the age of 18.[^cite_note-29] As a boy, Wojtyła was athletic, often playing [association football](10568.md) as [goalkeeper](5348091.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200625-30] During his childhood, Wojtyła had contact with the large Jewish community of [Wadowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadowice).[^cite_note-31] School football games were often organised between teams of Jews and Catholics, and Wojtyła often played on the Jewish side.[^cite_note-CNN6-26][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200625-30] In 2005, he recalled: "I remember that at least a third of my classmates at elementary school in Wadowice were Jews. At secondary school there were fewer. With some I was on very friendly terms. And what struck me about some of them was their Polish patriotism."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPope_John_Paul_II200599-32] It was around this time that the young Karol had his first serious relationship with a girl. He became close to a girl called Ginka Beer, described as "a Jewish beauty, with stupendous eyes and jet black hair, slender, a superb actress."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200632-33]

In mid-1938, Wojtyła and his father left Wadowice and moved to [Kraków](16815.md), where he enrolled at the [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University). While studying such topics as [philology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philology) and various languages, he worked as a volunteer librarian and though required to participate in [compulsory military training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_military_training) in the [Academic Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/36th_Infantry_Regiment_\(Poland\)), he refused to fire a weapon. He performed with various theatrical groups and worked as a playwright.[^cite_note-Kuhiwczak-34] During this time, his talent for language blossomed, and he learned as many as 15 languages – Polish, [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin), Italian, English, Spanish, Portuguese, French, German, [Luxembourgish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourgish), Dutch, Ukrainian, [Serbo-Croatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbo-Croatian), [Czech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_language), [Slovak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovak_language), and [Esperanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esperanto),[^cite_note-35] nine of which he used extensively as pope.

In 1939, after invading Poland, [Nazi Germany](21212.md)'s [occupation forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_Poland_\(1939%E2%80%931945\)) closed the university.[^cite_note-A\&E-24] Able-bodied males were required to work, so from 1940 to 1944 Wojtyła variously worked as a messenger for a restaurant, a manual labourer in a limestone quarry and for the [Solvay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solvay_\(company\)) chemical factory, to avoid deportation to Germany.[^cite_note-ShortBio-25][^cite_note-Kuhiwczak-34] In February 1940, he met [Jan Tyranowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Tyranowski), who introduced him to the [Carmelite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmelite) spirituality and the "[Living Rosary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Living_Rosary)" youth groups.[^cite_note-36] In that same year he had two major accidents, suffering a fractured skull after being struck by a tram and sustaining injuries which left him with one shoulder higher than the other and a permanent stoop after being hit by a lorry in the quarry.[^cite_note-37] His father, a former [Austro-Hungarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austro-Hungarian) [non-commissioned officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-commissioned_officer) and later officer in the [Polish Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Army), died of a heart attack in 1941,[^cite_note-38] leaving the young adult Wojtyła an orphan and the immediate family's only surviving member.[^cite_note-CNN6-26][^cite_note-CBN-27][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200660-39] Reflecting on these times of his life, nearly 40 years later he said: "I was not at my mother's death, I was not at my brother's death, I was not at my father's death. At twenty, I had already lost all the people I loved."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200660-39]



Wojtyła (second from right) in a [Baudienst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baudienst) forced labour work crew during the [occupation of Poland (1939–1945)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_Poland_\(1939%E2%80%931945\)), c. 1941

After his father's death, he started thinking seriously about the priesthood.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200663-40] In October 1942, while [World War II](32927.md) continued, he knocked on the door of the [Bishop's Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop's_Palace,_Krak%C3%B3w), and asked to study for the priesthood.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200663-40] Soon after, he began courses in the [clandestine underground seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Poland_during_World_War_II) run by the [Archbishop of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Krak%C3%B3w), the future Cardinal [Adam Stefan Sapieha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Stefan_Sapieha).[^cite_note-41] On 29 February 1944, Wojtyła was hit by a German truck. German [Wehrmacht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wehrmacht) [officers](36301328.md) tended to him and sent him to a hospital. He spent two weeks there recovering from a severe [concussion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concussion) and a shoulder injury. It seemed to him that this accident and his survival was a confirmation of his vocation. On 6 August 1944, a day known as "Black Sunday",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeigel2001b71-42] the [Gestapo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gestapo) rounded up young men in Kraków to curtail [the uprising there](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w_Uprising_\(1944\)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeigel2001b71-42] similar to the recent [uprising in Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Uprising).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies2004253–254-43][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeigel2001b71–21-44] Wojtyła escaped by hiding in the basement of his uncle's house at 10 Tyniecka Street, while the German troops searched above.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200663-40][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies2004253–254-43][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeigel2001b71–21-44] More than 8,000 men and boys were taken that day, while Wojtyła escaped to the Archbishop's residence,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200663-40][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeigel2001b71-42][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies2004253–254-43] where he remained until after the Germans had left.[^cite_note-CNN6-26][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200663-40][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies2004253–254-43]

On the night of 17 January 1945, the [Germans fled the city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vistula%E2%80%93Oder_Offensive), and the students reclaimed the ruined [seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seminary). Wojtyła and another seminarian volunteered for the task of clearing away piles of frozen excrement from the toilets.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeigel2001b75-45] Wojtyła also helped a 14-year-old Jewish refugee girl named Edith Zierer,[^cite_note-EdithZ-46] who had escaped from a Nazi [labour camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_camp) in [Częstochowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cz%C4%99stochowa).[^cite_note-EdithZ-46] Edith had collapsed on a railway platform, so Wojtyła carried her to a train and stayed with her throughout the journey to Kraków. She later credited Wojtyła with saving her life that day.[^cite_note-CNNLive-47][^cite_note-archive-48][^cite_note-IHT-49] [B'nai B'rith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'nai_B'rith) and other authorities have said that Wojtyła helped protect many other [Polish Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Jews) from the Nazis. During the [Nazi occupation of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_occupation_of_Poland), a Jewish family sent their son, Stanley Berger, to be hidden by a [Gentile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentile) Polish family. Berger's biological Jewish parents were killed in [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust), and after the war Berger's new Christian parents asked Karol Wojtyła to baptise the boy. Wojtyła refused, saying that the child should be raised in the Jewish faith of his birth parents and nation, not as a Catholic.[^cite_note-50] He did everything he could to ensure that Berger leave Poland to be raised by his Jewish relatives in the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-51] In April 2005, shortly after John Paul II's death, the Israeli government created a commission to honour the legacy of John Paul II. One of the honorifics proposed by a head of Italy's Jewish community, Emmanuele Pacifici was the medal of the [Righteous Among the Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Righteous_Among_the_Nations).[^cite_note-52] In Wojtyła's last book, *[Memory and Identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memory_and_Identity)*, he described the 12 years of the Nazi régime as "bestiality",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPope_John_Paul_II200516-53] quoting from the Polish theologian and philosopher [Konstanty Michalski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstanty_Michalski).[^cite_note-54]

## Priesthood



Wojtyła (centre) in 1949



Wojtyła in 1958

After finishing his studies at the seminary in Kraków, Wojtyła was [ordained](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordained) as a priest on [All Saints' Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Saints'_Day), 1 November 1946,[^cite_note-CBN-27] by the Archbishop of Kraków, Cardinal Adam Stefan Sapieha.[^cite_note-ShortBio-25][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200671-55][^cite_note-Vatican2-56] Sapieha sent Wojtyła to Rome's Pontifical International Athenaeum *Angelicum*, the future [Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_Saint_Thomas_Aquinas), to study under the French [Dominican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Order) friar [Reginald Garrigou-Lagrange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reginald_Garrigou-Lagrange) beginning on 26 November 1946. He resided in the [Belgian Pontifical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Pontifical_College) during this time, under rectorship of [Maximilien de Furstenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilien_de_Furstenberg).[^cite_note-57] Wojtyła earned a [licence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Licentiate_of_Sacred_Theology) in July 1947, passed his doctoral exam on 14 June 1948, and successfully defended his doctoral thesis titled *Doctrina de fide apud S. Ioannem a Cruce* (The Doctrine of Faith in St. [John of the Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_of_the_Cross)) in philosophy on 19 June 1948.[^cite_note-58] The *Angelicum* preserves the original copy of Wojtyła's typewritten thesis.[^cite_note-59] Among other courses at the *Angelicum*, Wojtyła studied Hebrew with the Dutch Dominican Peter G. Duncker, author of the *Compendium grammaticae linguae hebraicae biblicae*.[^cite_note-60]



The [Pontifical International Athenaeum *Angelicum*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_St._Thomas_Aquinas_\(Angelicum\)) in Rome, Italy

According to Wojtyła's fellow student, the future Austrian cardinal [Alfons Stickler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfons_Stickler), in 1947 during his sojourn at the *Angelicum*, Wojtyła visited [Padre Pio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padre_Pio), who heard his confession and told him that one day he would ascend to "the highest post in the Church".[^cite_note-61] Stickler added that Wojtyła believed that the prophecy was fulfilled when he became a cardinal.[^cite_note-62]

Wojtyła returned to Poland in the summer of 1948 for his first [pastoral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastoral) assignment in the village of [Niegowić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niegowi%C4%87), 24 kilometres (15 miles) from Kraków, at the Church of the Assumption. He arrived at Niegowić at harvest time, where his first action was to kneel and kiss the ground.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006233-63] He repeated this gesture, which he adopted from [John Vianney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Vianney),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006233-63] throughout his papacy.

In March 1949, Wojtyła was transferred to the parish of [Saint Florian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Florian's_Church) in Kraków. He taught ethics at Jagiellonian University and subsequently at the [Catholic University of Lublin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_University_of_Lublin). While teaching, he gathered a group of about 20 young people, who began to call themselves *Rodzinka*, the "little family". They met for prayer, philosophical discussion, and to help the blind and the sick. The group eventually grew to approximately 200 participants, and their activities expanded to include annual [skiing](28478.md) and [kayaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayak) trips.[^cite_note-USCCB_Bio-64]

In 1953, Wojtyła's habilitation thesis was accepted by the Faculty of Theology at the Jagiellonian University. In 1954, he earned a [Doctorate in Sacred Theology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctor_of_Sacred_Theology),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton200697-65] writing a dissertation titled "Reevaluation of the possibility of founding a Catholic ethic on the ethical system of [Max Scheler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Scheler)"[^cite_note-66] ([Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language): *Ocena możliwości zbudowania etyki chrześcijańskiej przy założeniach systemu Maksa Schelera*).[^cite_note-67] Scheler was a German philosopher who founded a broad [philosophical movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophical_movement) that emphasised the study of conscious experience. The [Polish Communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_People's_Republic) authorities abolished the Faculty of Theology at the Jagiellonian University, thereby preventing him from receiving the degree until 1957.[^cite_note-Vatican2-56] Wojtyła developed a theological approach, called [phenomenological Thomism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phenomenological_Thomism), that combined traditional Catholic [Thomism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomism) with the ideas of [personalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personalism), a philosophical approach deriving from phenomenology, which was popular among Catholic intellectuals in Kraków during Wojtyła's intellectual development. He translated Scheler's *Formalism and the Ethics of Substantive Values*.[^cite_note-68] In 1961, he coined "Thomistic Personalism" to describe Aquinas's philosophy.[^cite_note-69]



Wojtyła pictured during a [kayaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayaking) trip to the countryside with a group of students, c. 1960

During this period, Wojtyła wrote a series of articles in Kraków's Catholic newspaper, *[Tygodnik Powszechny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tygodnik_Powszechny)* (*Universal Weekly*), dealing with contemporary church issues.[^cite_note-Zenit5-70] He focused on creating original [literary work](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literary_work) during his first dozen years as a priest. War, life in the [Polish People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_People's_Republic), and his pastoral responsibilities all fed his poetry and plays. Wojtyła published his work under two pseudonyms, *Andrzej Jawień* and *Stanisław Andrzej Gruda*,[^cite_note-Kuhiwczak-34][^cite_note-Zenit5-70] to distinguish his literary from his religious writings (issued under his own name), and also so that his literary works would be considered on their own merits.[^cite_note-Kuhiwczak-34][^cite_note-Zenit5-70] In 1960, Wojtyła published the influential theological book *[Love and Responsibility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_and_Responsibility)*, a defence of traditional church teachings on marriage from a new philosophical standpoint.[^cite_note-Kuhiwczak-34][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWojtyła1981-71]

The aforementioned students regularly joined Wojtyła for hiking, skiing, bicycling, camping and kayaking, accompanied by prayer, outdoor Masses, and theological discussions. In Stalinist-era Poland, it was not permitted for priests to travel with groups of students. Wojtyła asked his younger companions to call him "Wujek" (Polish for "Uncle") to prevent outsiders from deducing he was a priest. The nickname gained popularity among his followers. In 1958, when Wojtyła was named [auxiliary bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auxiliary_bishop) of Kraków, his acquaintances expressed concern that this would cause him to change. Wojtyła responded to his friends, "Wujek will remain Wujek", and he continued to live a simple life, shunning the trappings that came with his position as bishop. This beloved nickname stayed with Wojtyła for his entire life and continues to be affectionately used, particularly by the Polish people.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73]

## Episcopate and cardinalate

### Call to the episcopate



19 [Kanonicza Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanonicza_Street,_Krak%C3%B3w) in [Kraków](16815.md), Poland, where John Paul II lived as a priest and bishop (now an [Archdiocese Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocesal_Museum_in_Krak%C3%B3w))

On 4 July 1958,[^cite_note-Vatican2-56] while Wojtyła was on a kayaking holiday in the lakes region of northern Poland, [Pope Pius XII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII) appointed him as an [auxiliary bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auxiliary_bishop) of Kraków. He was consequently summoned to [Warsaw](32908.md) to meet the [Primate](24672.md) of Poland, Cardinal [Stefan Wyszyński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Wyszy%C5%84ski), who informed him of his appointment.[^cite_note-Rise-74][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton2006103-75] Wojtyła accepted the appointment as auxiliary bishop to Kraków's Archbishop [Eugeniusz Baziak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugeniusz_Baziak), and he received episcopal consecration (as [titular bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titular_bishop) of [Ombi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ombi)) on 28 September 1958, with Baziak as the principal consecrator and as co-consecrators Bishop [Bolesław Kominek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boles%C5%82aw_Kominek) (titular bishop of [Sophene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophene)), auxiliary of the Catholic [Archdiocese of Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Wroc%C5%82aw), and [Franciszek Jop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciszek_Jop), [Auxiliary Bishop of Sandomierz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Sandomierz) (Titular Bishop of [Daulia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daulia)). Kominek was to become Cardinal [Archbishop of Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Wroc%C5%82aw) and Jop was later Auxiliary Bishop of Wrocław and then Bishop of [Opole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Opole).[^cite_note-Vatican2-56] At the age of 38, Wojtyła became the youngest bishop in Poland.

In 1959, Wojtyła began an annual tradition of saying a [Midnight Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnight_Mass) on Christmas Day in an open field at [Nowa Huta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nowa_Huta), the so-called model workers' town outside Kraków that was without a church building.[^cite_note-76] Baziak died in June 1962 and on 16 July, Wojtyła was selected as *[Vicar Capitular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicar_Capitular)* *(temporary administrator)* of the Archdiocese until an [archbishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop) could be appointed.[^cite_note-A\&E-24][^cite_note-ShortBio-25]

### Participation in Vatican II and subsequent events

From October 1962, Wojtyła took part in the [Second Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council) (1962–1965),[^cite_note-A\&E-24][^cite_note-Vatican2-56] where he made contributions to two of its most historic and influential products, the *Decree on Religious Freedom* (in Latin, *[Dignitatis humanae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dignitatis_humanae)*) and the *Pastoral Constitution on the Church in the Modern World* (*[Gaudium et spes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaudium_et_spes)*).[^cite_note-Vatican2-56] Wojtyła and the Polish bishops contributed a draft text to the Council for *Gaudium et spes*. According to the [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit) historian [John W. O'Malley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_W._O'Malley), the draft text *Gaudium et spes* that Wojtyła and the Polish delegation sent "had some influence on the version that was sent to the council fathers that summer but was not accepted as the base text".[^cite_note-77] According to [John F. Crosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Crosby_\(philosopher\)), as pope, John Paul II used the words of *Gaudium et spes* later to introduce his own views on the nature of the human person in relation to God: man is "the only creature on earth that God has wanted for its own sake", but man "can fully discover his true self only in a sincere giving of himself".[^cite_note-78]

Wojtyła also participated in the assemblies of the [Synod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod) of Bishops.[^cite_note-A\&E-24][^cite_note-ShortBio-25] On 13 January 1964, [Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI) appointed him [Archbishop of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Krak%C3%B3w).[^cite_note-ShortBio-25] On 26 June 1967, Paul VI announced Wojtyła's promotion to the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals).[^cite_note-Vatican2-56][^cite_note-ShortBio-25] Wojtyła was named [cardinal priest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_priest) of the [titular church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titular_church) of [San Cesareo in Palatio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Cesareo_in_Palatio).



[Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI) imposing the cardinal biretta on Wojtyła in 1967

In 1967, he was instrumental in formulating the [encyclical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclical) *[Humanae vitae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanae_vitae)*, which dealt with the same issues that forbid abortion and [artificial birth control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_birth_control).[^cite_note-Vatican2-56][^cite_note-Memory-79][^cite_note-HV-80]

According to a contemporary witness, Wojtyła was against the distribution of a letter around Kraków in 1970, stating that the Polish Episcopate was preparing for the 50th anniversary of the [Polish–Soviet War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Soviet_War).[^cite_note-81]

In 1973, Wojtyła met philosopher [Anna-Teresa Tymieniecka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Teresa_Tymieniecka), the wife of [Hendrik S. Houthakker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendrik_S._Houthakker), professor of economics at [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University) and [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University), and member of President [Richard Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon)'s [Council of Economic Advisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Economic_Advisers)[^cite_note-Stourton-82][^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83][^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84] Tymieniecka collaborated with Wojtyła on a number of projects including an English translation of Wojtyła's book [*Osoba i czyn*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II_bibliography) (*Person and Act*). *Person and Act*, one of John Paul II's foremost literary works, was initially written in Polish.[^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83] Tymieniecka produced the English-language version.[^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83] They corresponded over the years, and grew to be good friends.[^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83][^cite_note-Panarama-85] When Wojtyła visited New England in the summer of 1976, Tymieniecka put him up as a guest in her family home.[^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83][^cite_note-Panarama-85] Wojtyła enjoyed his holiday in [Pomfret, Vermont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pomfret,_Vermont), kayaking and enjoying the outdoors, as he had done in his beloved Poland.[^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton2006103-75]

During 1974–1975, Wojtyła served [Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI) as consultor to the [Pontifical Council for the Laity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Council_for_the_Laity), as recording secretary for the 1974 synod on evangelism and by participating extensively in the original drafting of the 1975 [apostolic exhortation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_exhortation), *[Evangelii nuntiandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelii_nuntiandi)*.[^cite_note-86]

## Papacy

### Election

Main article: [October 1978 papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_1978_papal_conclave)



First appearance of Pope John Paul II following his election on 16 October 1978

In August 1978, following the death of Pope Paul VI, Wojtyła voted in [the papal conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_1978_papal_conclave), which elected [John Paul I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_I). John Paul I died after only 33 days as pope, triggering another [conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conclave).[^cite_note-ShortBio-25][^cite_note-Vatican2-56][^cite_note-Time1978-87]

The second conclave of 1978 started on 14 October, ten days after the funeral. It was split between two strong [candidates for the papacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papabile): Cardinal [Giuseppe Siri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Siri), the conservative [Archbishop of Genoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Genoa), and Cardinal [Giovanni Benelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Benelli), the liberal [Archbishop of Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Florence) and a close friend of John Paul I.[^cite_note-Time1978b-88]



The [coat of arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms) of John Paul II displaying the *[Marian Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marian_Cross)* with the letter M signifying the [Blessed Virgin Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary), the mother of Jesus

Supporters of Benelli were confident that he would be elected, and in early [ballots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballot), Benelli came within nine votes of success.[^cite_note-Time1978b-88] However, both men faced sufficient opposition for neither to be likely to prevail. [Giovanni Colombo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Colombo), the [Archbishop of Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Milan), was considered as a compromise candidate among the Italian cardinal-electors, but when he started to receive votes, he announced that, if elected, he would decline to accept the papacy.[^cite_note-89] Cardinal [Franz König](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_K%C3%B6nig), [Archbishop of Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Vienna), suggested Wojtyła as another compromise candidate to his fellow electors.[^cite_note-Time1978b-88] Wojtyła won on the eighth ballot on the third day (16 October).

Among those cardinals who rallied behind Wojtyła were supporters of Giuseppe Siri, [Stefan Wyszyński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Wyszy%C5%84ski), most of the American cardinals (led by [John Krol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Krol)), and other moderate cardinals. He accepted his election with the words: "With obedience in faith to Christ, my Lord, and with trust in the Mother of Christ and the Church, in spite of great difficulties, I accept".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton2006171-90][^cite_note-NewPope-91] The Pope, in tribute to his immediate predecessor, then took the [papal name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_name) of *John Paul II*,[^cite_note-Vatican2-56][^cite_note-Time1978b-88] also in honour of the late Popes Paul VI and John XXIII, and the traditional white smoke informed the crowd gathered in [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square) that a pope had been chosen. There had been rumours that the new pope wished to be known as *Pope Stanislaus* in honour of [the Polish saint of the name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislaus_of_Szczepan%C3%B3w), but was convinced by the cardinals that it was not a Roman name.[^cite_note-Time1978-87] When the new pontiff appeared on the balcony, he broke tradition by addressing the gathered crowd:[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton2006171-90]

> "Dear brothers and sisters, we are saddened at the death of our beloved Pope John Paul I, and so the cardinals have called for a new bishop of Rome. They called him from a faraway land—far and yet always close because of our communion in faith and Christian traditions. I was afraid to accept that responsibility, yet I do so in a spirit of obedience to the Lord and total faithfulness to Mary, our most Holy Mother. I am speaking to you in your—no, our Italian language. If I make a mistake, please *corrict* \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*][^cite_note-92]  me."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton2006171-90][^cite_note-93][^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-95]

Wojtyła became the 264th pope according to the chronological [list of popes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_popes), and the first non-Italian in 455 years.[^cite_note-upi-96] At only 58 years of age, he was the youngest pope since [Pope Pius IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX) in 1846, who was 54.[^cite_note-Vatican2-56] Like his predecessor, John Paul II dispensed with the traditional [papal coronation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_coronation) and instead received ecclesiastical [investiture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investiture) with a simplified [papal inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_inauguration) on 22 October 1978. During his [inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_inauguration_of_Pope_John_Paul_II), when the cardinals were to kneel before him to take their vows and kiss his ring, he stood up as the Polish prelate, Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński knelt down, stopped him from kissing the ring, and simply embraced him.[^cite_note-Vatican-bio-en-97]

### Pastoral journeys

Main article: [List of pastoral trips made by Pope John Paul II](1466335.md)



John Paul's first papal trip to Poland in June 1979

During his pontificate, John Paul II made journeys to 129 countries,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006234-98] travelling more than 1,100,000 kilometres (680,000 mi) while doing so. He consistently attracted large crowds, some among the largest ever assembled in [human history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_history), such as the Manila [World Youth Day 1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Youth_Day_1995), which gathered up to four million people, the largest papal gathering ever, according to the Vatican.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-AsiaNews-100] John Paul II's earliest official visits were to the Dominican Republic and Mexico in January 1979.[^cite_note-CBN2-101] While some of his journeys (such as to the United States and the [Holy Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Land)) were to places previously visited by Pope Paul VI, John Paul II became the first pope to visit the [White House](33057.md) in October 1979, where he was [greeted warmly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_meetings_between_the_pope_and_the_president_of_the_United_States) by President [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter). He was the first pope ever to visit several countries in one year, starting in 1979 with Mexico[^cite_note-Mexico-102] and [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II's_visit_to_Ireland).[^cite_note-Ireland-103] He was the first reigning pope to [travel to the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II's_visit_to_the_United_Kingdom), in 1982, where he met [Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_II), the [Supreme Governor of the Church of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Governor_of_the_Church_of_England). While in Britain he also visited [Canterbury Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canterbury_Cathedral) and knelt in prayer with [Robert Runcie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Runcie), the [Archbishop of Canterbury](2345.md), at the spot where [Thomas Becket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Becket) had been killed,[^cite_note-104] as well as holding several large-scale open air Masses, including one at [Wembley Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wembley_Stadium_\(1923\)), which was attended by some 80,000 people.[^cite_note-105]



John Paul II with [Sandro Pertini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Pertini), [President of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Italy), in 1984

He travelled to Haiti in 1983, where he spoke in [Creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haitian_Creole) to thousands of impoverished Catholics gathered to greet him at the airport. His message, "things must change in Haiti", referring to the disparity between the wealthy and the poor, was met with thunderous applause.[^cite_note-Haiti:_The_Duvalier_Years-106] In 2000, he was the first modern pope to visit Egypt,[^cite_note-PopeEgypt-107] where he met the [Coptic pope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church), [Pope Shenouda III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Shenouda_III_of_Alexandria),[^cite_note-PopeEgypt-107] and the [Greek Orthodox Patriarch of Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Patriarch_of_Alexandria).[^cite_note-PopeEgypt-107] He was the first Catholic pope to visit and pray in an Islamic mosque, in [Damascus](8914.md), Syria, in 2001. He visited the [Umayyad Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umayyad_Mosque), a former Christian church where [John the Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_the_Baptist) is believed to be interred,[^cite_note-Mosque-108] where he made a speech calling for Muslims, Christians and Jews to live together.[^cite_note-Mosque-108]

On 15 January 1995, during the X World Youth Day, he offered [Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Mass) to an estimated crowd of between five and seven million in [Luneta Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luneta_Park),[^cite_note-AsiaNews-100] [Manila](184334.md), Philippines, which was considered to be the largest single gathering in [Christian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_history).[^cite_note-AsiaNews-100] In March 2000, while visiting [Jerusalem](16043.md), John Paul became the first pope in history to visit and pray at the [Western Wall](75225.md).[^cite_note-BBCIsrael-109][^cite_note-ADL2006-110] In September 2001, amid post-[11 September](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/11_September_attacks) concerns, he travelled to Kazakhstan, with an audience largely consisting of Muslims, and to Armenia, to participate in the celebration of 1,700 years of [Armenian Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church) as well as honor the victims of the [Armenian genocide](140376.md).[^cite_note-NewYorkTimes3-111][^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113]

In June 1979, John Paul II travelled to Poland, where ecstatic crowds constantly surrounded him.[^cite_note-OnThisDay-114] This first papal trip to Poland and the enthusiastic response to it contributed to the formation of the [Solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_\(Polish_trade_union\)) movement in 1980, which later played a major role in the [end of socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989) in Poland.[^cite_note-Memory-79] Leaders of the [Polish United Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_United_Workers'_Party) intended to use the Pope's visit to show the people that although the Pope was Polish, it did not alter their capacity to govern, oppress, and distribute the goods of society. They also hoped that if the Pope abided by the rules they set, the Polish people would see his example and follow them as well. If the Pope's visit inspired a riot, the Communist leaders of Poland were prepared to crush the uprising and blame the suffering on the Pope.[^cite_note-Angelo_M._Codevilla_2008-115]

> The Pope won that struggle by transcending politics. His was what [Joseph Nye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Nye) calls '[soft power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_power)' — the power of attraction and repulsion. He began with an enormous advantage, and exploited it to the utmost: He headed the one institution that stood for the polar opposite of the Communist way of life that the Polish people hated. He was a Pole, but beyond the regime's reach. By identifying with him, Poles would have the chance to cleanse themselves of the compromises they had to make to live under the regime. And so they came to him by the millions. They listened. He told them to be good, not to compromise themselves, to stick by one another, to be fearless, and that God is the only source of goodness, the only standard of conduct. 'Be not afraid,' he said. Millions shouted in response, 'We want God! We want God! We want God!' The regime cowered. Had the Pope chosen to turn his soft power into the hard variety, the regime might have been drowned in blood. Instead, the Pope simply led the Polish people to desert their rulers by affirming solidarity with one another. The Communists managed to [hold on as despots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law_in_Poland) a decade longer. But as political leaders, they were finished. Visiting his native Poland in 1979, Pope John Paul II struck what turned out to be a mortal blow to its Communist regime, to the Soviet Empire, \[and] ultimately to Communism.[^cite_note-Angelo_M._Codevilla_2008-115]

> "When Pope John Paul II kissed the ground at the Warsaw airport he began the process by which Communism in Poland—and ultimately elsewhere in Europe—would come to an end."[^cite_note-116]

On later trips to Poland, he gave tacit support to the Solidarity organisation.[^cite_note-Memory-79] These visits reinforced this message and contributed to the collapse of East European Communism that took place between 1989 and 1990 with the reintroduction of democracy in Poland, and which then spread through Eastern Europe (1990–1991) and South-Eastern Europe (1990–1992).[^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006234-98][^cite_note-OnThisDay-114][^cite_note-CBCNews-117][^cite_note-Gorbachev-118]

### World Youth Days



Pope John Paul II (right) with Cardinal [Jaime Sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaime_Sin), [Archbishop of Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Manila) (left), addressing the crowd attending the closing Mass of the tenth [World Youth Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Youth_Day_1995) at [Luneta Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luneta_Park), 1995

As an extension of his successful work with youth as a young priest, John Paul II pioneered the international [World Youth Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Youth_Day). John Paul II presided over nine of them: [Rome](25458.md) (1985 and 2000), [Buenos Aires](255919.md) (1987), [Santiago de Compostela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_de_Compostela) (1989), [Częstochowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cz%C4%99stochowa) (1991), [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver) (1993), [Manila](184334.md) (1995), [Paris](22989.md) (1997), and [Toronto](64646.md) (2002). Total attendance at these signature events of the pontificate was in the tens of millions.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-119]

John Paul II said in Ireland that: "I believe in youth with all my heart and with all the strength of my conviction." When he visited [New York City](645042.md), he was given a T-shirt, a pair of jeans, a guitar and medal on a chain, symbols associated with a modern, American young adulthood.[^cite_note-120]

### Dedicated years

Keenly aware of the rhythms of time and the importance of anniversaries in the Catholic Church's life, John Paul II led nine "dedicated years" during the twenty-six and a half years of his pontificate: the Holy Year of the Redemption in 1983–84, the [Marian Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marian_Year) in 1987–88, the Year of the Family in 1993–94, the three Trinitarian years of preparation for the [Great Jubilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Jubilee) of 2000, the Great Jubilee itself, the Year of the Rosary in 2002–03, and the [Year of the Eucharist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Year_of_the_Eucharist), which began on 17 October 2004, and concluded six months after the Pope's death.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-119]

### Music albums

John Paul II recorded music albums. In 1979, his album *Pope John Paul II sings at the Festival of Sacrosong* was recorded by [Infinity Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infinity_Records).[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122] In 1994 he released a music album title *The Rosary*.[^cite_note-123] In 1999, John Paul II released another music album titled *[Abba Pater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abba_Pater)*.[^cite_note-124]

### Great Jubilee of 2000

The [Great Jubilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Jubilee) of 2000 was a call to the church to become more aware and to embrace her missionary task for the work of [evangelization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_evangelization):

> From the beginning of my Pontificate, my thoughts had been on this Holy Year 2000 as an important appointment. I thought of its celebration as a providential opportunity during which the Church, thirty-five years after the Second Vatican Ecumenical Council, would examine how far she had renewed herself, in order to be able to take up her evangelising mission with fresh enthusiasm.[^cite_note-125]

John Paul II also made a pilgrimage to the [Holy Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Land) for the Great Jubilee of 2000.[^cite_note-Vatican3-126] During his visit to the Holy Land, John Paul II visited many sites of the [Rosary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosary), including the following locations: [Bethany Beyond the Jordan (Al-Maghtas)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Maghtas), at the [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River), where [John the Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_the_Baptist) baptized Jesus; [Manger Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manger_Square) and the [Church of the Nativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Nativity) in the town of [Bethlehem](4312.md), the location of Jesus' birth; and the [Church of the Holy Sepulchre](7810.md) in [Jerusalem](16043.md), the site of Jesus' burial and resurrection.[^cite_note-Vatican5-127][^cite_note-Vatican6-128][^cite_note-Vatican7-129]
