As pope, John Paul II wrote [14 papal encyclicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_encyclicals_of_Pope_John_Paul_II) and taught regularly in his general [audiences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audience_\(meeting\)#Holy_See).[^cite_note-130]

Some key elements of his strategy to "reposition the Catholic Church" were encyclicals such as *[Ecclesia de Eucharistia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesia_de_Eucharistia)*, *[Reconciliatio et paenitentia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconciliatio_et_paenitentia)* and *[Redemptoris Mater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redemptoris_Mater)*. In his *At the beginning of the new millennium* (*[Novo Millennio Ineunte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novo_Millennio_Ineunte)*), he emphasised the importance of "starting afresh from Christ": "No, we shall not be saved by a formula but by a Person." In *The Splendour of the Truth* (*[Veritatis Splendor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veritatis_Splendor)*), he emphasised the dependence of man on God and His Law ("Without the Creator, the creature disappears") and the "dependence of freedom on the truth". He warned that man "giving himself over to relativism and scepticism, goes off in search of an illusory freedom apart from truth itself". In *[Fides et Ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fides_et_Ratio)* (*On the Relationship between Faith and Reason*) John Paul promoted a renewed interest in philosophy and an autonomous pursuit of truth in theological matters. Drawing on many different sources (such as Thomism), he described the mutually supporting relationship between [faith and reason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faith_and_reason), and emphasised that theologians should focus on that relationship. John Paul II wrote extensively about workers and the [social doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_social_teaching) of the church, which he discussed in three encyclicals: *[Laborem exercens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laborem_exercens)*, *[Sollicitudo rei socialis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sollicitudo_rei_socialis)*, and *[Centesimus annus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centesimus_annus)*. Through his encyclicals and many [Apostolic Letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_epistles) and Exhortations, John Paul II talked about the [dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dignity) and the equality of women.[^cite_note-131] He argued for the importance of the family for the future of humanity.[^cite_note-Memory-79] He taught about sexuality in what is referred as the "[Theology of the Body](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theology_of_the_Body)". Other encyclicals include *The Gospel of Life* (*[Evangelium Vitae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelium_Vitae)*) and *[Ut unum sint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ut_unum_sint)* (*That They May Be One*). Though critics accused him of inflexibility in explicitly re-asserting Catholic moral teachings against abortion and [euthanasia](9587.md) that have been in place for well over a thousand years, he urged a more nuanced view of [capital punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_and_capital_punishment).[^cite_note-Memory-79] In his second encyclical, *[Dives in misericordia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dives_in_misericordia)*, he stressed that [divine mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_mercy) is the greatest feature of God, needed especially in modern times.

## Promulgation of 1983 Code of Canon Law and 1992 Catechism of the Catholic Church

Main article: [Canon law (Catholic Church)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_law_\(Catholic_Church\))

John Paul II completed a full-scale reform of the Catholic Church's legal system, Latin and Eastern, and a reform of the Roman Curia.

On 18 October 1990, when promulgating the *[Code of Canons of the Eastern Churches](3547385.md)*, John Paul II stated

> By the publication of this Code, the canonical ordering of the whole Church is thus at length completed, following as it does...the "[*Apostolic Constitution on the Roman Curia*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastor_Bonus)" of 1988, which is added to both Codes as the primary instrument of the Roman Pontiff for 'the communion that binds together, as it were, the whole Church'[^cite_note-132]

In 1998, John Paul II issued the [motu proprio](793396.md) *[Ad tuendam fidem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad_tuendam_fidem)*, which amended two canons (750 and 1371) of the 1983 Code of Canon Law and two canons (598 and 1436) of the 1990 Code of Canons of the Eastern Churches.

### 1983 *Code of Canon Law*

Main article: [1983 Code of Canon Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Code_of_Canon_Law)

On 25 January 1983, with the [apostolic constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_constitution) *[Sacrae disciplinae leges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrae_disciplinae_leges)* John Paul II promulgated the current code of canon law for all members of the Catholic Church who belonged to the [Latin Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church). It entered into force the first Sunday of the following [Advent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advent),[^cite_note-sacrae-133] which was 27 November 1983.[^cite_note-134] John Paul II described the new code as "the last document of Vatican II".[^cite_note-sacrae-133] [Edward N. Peters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_N._Peters) has referred to the 1983 *Code* as the "Johanno-Pauline Code"[^cite_note-135] (*Johannes Paulus* is [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin) for "John Paul"), parallelling the ["Pio-Benedictine" 1917 code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1917_Code_of_Canon_Law) that it replaced.

### *Code of Canons of the Eastern Churches*

Main article: [Code of Canons of the Eastern Churches](3547385.md)

John Paul II promulgated the *[Code of Canons of the Eastern Churches](3547385.md)* (CCEO) on 18 October 1990, by the document *Sacri Canones*.[^cite_note-136] The CCEO came into force of law on 1 October 1991.[^cite_note-137] It is the [codification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codification_\(law\)) of the common portions of the [canon law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_law_of_the_Catholic_Church) for the 23 of the 24 *[sui iuris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sui_iuris)* churches in the [Catholic Church](606848.md) that are the [Eastern Catholic Churches](214903.md). It is divided into 30 titles and has a total of 1540 [canons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_\(canon_law\)).[^cite_note-138]

### *Pastor bonus*

Main article: [Pastor bonus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastor_bonus)

John Paul II promulgated the apostolic constitution *[Pastor bonus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastor_bonus)* on 28 June 1988. It instituted a number of reforms in the process of running the [Roman Curia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Curia). *Pastor bonus* laid out in considerable detail the organisation of the Roman Curia, specifying precisely the names and composition of each dicastery, and enumerating the competencies of each [dicastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicastery). It replaced the previous special law, *Regimini Ecclesiæ universæ*, which was promulgated by Paul VI in 1967.[^cite_note-139]

### *Catechism of the Catholic Church*

Main article: [Catechism of the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_the_Catholic_Church)

On 11 October 1992, in his apostolic constitution *[Fidei depositum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidei_depositum)* (*The Deposit of Faith*), John Paul ordered the publication of the *[Catechism of the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_the_Catholic_Church)*.[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141]

He declared the publication to be "a sure norm for teaching the faith … a sure and authentic reference text for teaching Catholic doctrine and particularly for preparing local catechisms". It was "meant to encourage and assist in the writing of new local catechisms \[both applicable and faithful]" rather than replacing them.[^cite_note-142]

## Political views and activity

Main article: [Political views of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_views_of_Pope_John_Paul_II)

### Anti-communism

See also: [Holy See–Soviet Union relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Soviet_Union_relations)

#### Role as spiritual inspiration and catalyst

By the late 1970s, the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) had been [predicted by some observers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predictions_of_the_collapse_of_the_Soviet_Union).[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] John Paul II has been credited with being instrumental in bringing down Communism in Central and Eastern Europe,[^cite_note-Memory-79][^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006234-98][^cite_note-CBCNews-117][^cite_note-Gorbachev-118][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDomínguez2005-145] by being the spiritual inspiration behind its downfall and catalyst for "a peaceful revolution" in Poland. [Lech Wałęsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lech_Wa%C5%82%C4%99sa), the founder of [Solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_\(Polish_trade_union\)) and the first [post-Communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Communist) [President of Poland](43581.md), credited John Paul II with giving Poles the courage to demand change.[^cite_note-Memory-79] According to Wałęsa, "Before his pontificate, the world was divided into blocs. Nobody knew how to get rid of Communism. In [Warsaw](32908.md), in 1979, he simply said: 'Do not be afraid', and later prayed: 'Let your Spirit descend and change the image of the land ... this land'."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDomínguez2005-145] It has also been widely alleged that the [Vatican Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_Bank) covertly funded Solidarity.[^cite_note-NYTimes-146][^cite_note-Salinger2005-147]



US President [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) meeting with Pope John Paul II during a visit to the [Vatican City](32408.md), 1982

In 1984, the [foreign policy of the Ronald Reagan administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_Ronald_Reagan_administration) saw opened diplomatic relations with the Vatican [for the first time since 1870](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93United_States_relations). In sharp contrast to the long history of strong domestic opposition, this time there was very little opposition from Congress, the courts, and Protestant groups.[^cite_note-148] Relations between Reagan and John Paul II were close, especially because of their shared [anti-communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communism) and keen interest in forcing the Soviets out of Poland.[^cite_note-149] John Paul II and Reagan had earlier confided in the Vatican (in 1982) "their conviction that God had spared their lives" from assassination "for the divine purpose of defeating the communist empire."[^cite_note-150] Reagan's correspondence with the Pope reveals "a continuous scurrying to shore up Vatican support for U.S. policies. Perhaps most surprisingly, the papers show that, as late as 1984, the Pope did not believe the Communist Polish government could be changed."[^cite_note-nationalreview-151]

> "No one can prove conclusively that he was a primary cause of the end of communism. However, the major figures on all sides—not just Lech Wałęsa, the Polish Solidarity leader, but also Solidarity's arch-opponent, General [Wojciech Jaruzelski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wojciech_Jaruzelski); not just the former American president [George Bush Senior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Bush_Senior) but also the former Soviet president Mikhail Gorbachev—now agree that he was. I would argue the historical case in three steps: without the Polish Pope, no Solidarity revolution in Poland in 1980; without Solidarity, no dramatic change in Soviet policy towards eastern Europe under Gorbachev; without that change, no velvet revolutions in 1989."[^cite_note-152]

In December 1989, John Paul II met the Soviet leader [Mikhail Gorbachev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev) at the Vatican and each expressed his respect and admiration for the other. Gorbachev once said: "The collapse of the [Iron Curtain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Curtain) would have been impossible without John Paul II."[^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-CBCNews-117] On John Paul II's death, Gorbachev said: "Pope John Paul II's devotion to his followers is a remarkable example to all of us."[^cite_note-Gorbachev-118][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDomínguez2005-145]



Pope John Paul II, given the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom) by US President [George W. Bush](3414021.md) in 2004

On 4 June 2004, U.S. president [George W. Bush](3414021.md) presented the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom), the United States' highest civilian honour, to John Paul II during a ceremony at the [Apostolic Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Palace). The president read the citation that accompanied the medal, which recognised "this son of Poland" whose "principled stand for peace and freedom has inspired millions and helped to topple communism and tyranny".[^cite_note-153] After receiving the award, John Paul II said, "May the desire for freedom, peace, a more humane world symbolised by this medal inspire men and women of goodwill in every time and place."[^cite_note-vatican1-154]

#### Communist attempt to compromise John Paul II



Graffiti showing John Paul II with quote "Do not be afraid" in [Rijeka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rijeka), Croatia

In 1983, Poland's Communist government unsuccessfully tried to humiliate John Paul II by falsely saying he had fathered an illegitimate child. Section D of [Służba Bezpieczeństwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C5%82u%C5%BCba_Bezpiecze%C5%84stwa) (SB), the security service, had an action named "Triangolo" to carry out criminal operations against the [Catholic Church in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Poland); the operation encompassed all Polish hostile actions against the Pope.[^cite_note-155] Captain Grzegorz Piotrowski, one of the murderers of beatified [Jerzy Popiełuszko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerzy_Popie%C5%82uszko), was the leader of section D. They drugged Irena Kinaszewska, the secretary of the Kraków-based weekly Catholic magazine *[Tygodnik Powszechny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tygodnik_Powszechny)* where Wojtyła had worked, and unsuccessfully attempted to make her admit to having had sexual relations with him.[^cite_note-sbd-156]

The SB then attempted to compromise Kraków priest Andrzej Bardecki, an editor of *Tygodnik Powszechny* and one of the closest friends of Cardinal Wojtyła before he became pope, by planting false memoirs in his dwelling; Piotrowski was exposed and the forgeries were found and destroyed before the SB could say to have discovered them.[^cite_note-sbd-156]

### Latin American and Caribbean dictatorships

John Paul II was variously praised[^cite_note-157] and criticized[^cite_note-158] for actions which were perceived as both inspiring resistance[^cite_note-159] but also potentially abetting[^cite_note-160] dictatorships in [Chile](5489.md), [Haiti](13373.md), and [Paraguay](23420.md).

## Relations with other Christian denominations and religions

Main articles: [Pope John Paul II and ecumenism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II_and_ecumenism) and [Pope John Paul II's relations with the Eastern Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II's_relations_with_the_Eastern_Orthodox_Church)

### Christian denominations

John Paul II was publicly committed to [improving relationships between Christian communities](146656.md) and engaged in numerous dialogues with leaders of other Christian churches, including the [Eastern Orthodox Church](10186.md),[^cite_note-161] [Oriental Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriental_Orthodox_Church),[^cite_note-162] [Assyrian Church of the East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_Church_of_the_East)[^cite_note-163] the [Lutheran World Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_World_Federation),[^cite_note-164] and the [Anglican Communion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Communion).[^cite_note-165]

### Religions

#### Judaism

Main article: [Pope John Paul II and Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II_and_Judaism)

[Relations between Catholicism and Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relations_between_Catholicism_and_Judaism) improved dramatically during the pontificate of John Paul II.[^cite_note-Memory-79][^cite_note-ADL2006-110] He spoke frequently about the Catholic Church's relationship with the Jewish faith.[^cite_note-Memory-79]

#### Animism

In his book-length interview *[Crossing the Threshold of Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crossing_the_Threshold_of_Hope)* with the Italian journalist [Vittorio Messori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vittorio_Messori) published in 1995, John Paul II draws parallels between [animism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism) and Christianity. He wrote:

> "... it would be helpful to recall ... the animist religions which stress ancestor worship. It seems that those who practise them are particularly close to Christianity, and among them, the Church's missionaries also find it easier to speak a common language. Is there, perhaps, in this veneration of ancestors a kind of preparation for the Christian faith in the Communion of Saints, in which all believers—whether living or dead—form a single community, a single body? ... There is nothing strange, then, that the African and Asian animists would become believers in Christ more easily than followers of the great religions of the Far East."[^cite_note-166]

In 1985, the Pope visited the African country of [Togo](30138.md), where 60 per cent of the population espouses animist beliefs. To honour the Pope, animist religious leaders met him at a Catholic Marian shrine in the forest, much to the pontiff's delight. John Paul II proceeded to call for the need for religious tolerance, praised nature, and emphasised common elements between animism and Christianity, saying:

> "Nature, exuberant and splendid in this area of forests and lakes, impregnates spirits and hearts with its mystery and orients them spontaneously toward the mystery of He who is the author of life. It is this religious sentiment that animates you and one can say that animates all of your compatriots."[^cite_note-167]

During the investiture of President [Thomas Boni Yayi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Boni_Yayi) of [Benin](3459.md) as a titled [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people) [chieftain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Chieftaincy) on 20 December 2008, the reigning [Ooni of Ile-Ife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ooni_of_Ile-Ife), Nigeria, [Olubuse II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olubuse_II), referred to John Paul II as a previous recipient of the same royal honour.[^cite_note-168]

#### Buddhism

Tenzin Gyatso, the [14th Dalai Lama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/14th_Dalai_Lama), visited John Paul II eight times. The two men held many similar views and understood similar plights, both coming from nations affected by Communism and both serving as heads of major religious bodies.[^cite_note-Dalai-169][^cite_note-Lama-170] As Archbishop of Kraków, long before the 14th Dalai Lama was a world-famous figure, Wojtyła held special Masses to pray for the Tibetan people's non-violent struggle for freedom from [Maoist China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maoist_China).[^cite_note-171] In 1987, he welcomed participants of the *East-West Spiritual Exchanges*, an initiative by the [Monastic Interreligious Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monastic_Interreligious_Dialogue) (DIMMID) and the Institute for Zen Studies in which Buddhist and Christian monks or nuns take turns residing for one month in each other's monasteries.[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173] During his 1995 visit to [Sri Lanka](26750.md), a country where a majority of the population adheres to [Theravada Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theravada_Buddhism), John Paul II expressed his admiration for Buddhism. He said:

> "In particular I express my highest regard for the followers of Buddhism, the majority religion in Sri Lanka, with its ... four great values of … loving kindness, compassion, sympathetic joy and equanimity; with its ten transcendental virtues and the joys of the [Sangha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangha) expressed so beautifully in the Theragathas. I ardently hope that my visit will serve to strengthen the goodwill between us, and that it will reassure everyone of the Catholic Church's desire for interreligious dialogue and cooperation in building a more just and fraternal world. To everyone I extend the hand of friendship, recalling the splendid words of the [Dhammapada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhammapada): 'Better than a thousand useless words is one single word that gives peace' ... ."[^cite_note-174]

#### Islam



John Paul II was the first Pope to enter and pray in a mosque, visiting the tomb of John the Baptist at [Umayyad Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umayyad_Mosque), Damascus.

John Paul II made considerable efforts to improve relations between Catholicism and Islam.[^cite_note-Crossing_the_Threshold_of_Hope-175]

He officially supported the project of the [Mosque of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosque_of_Rome) and participated in the inauguration in 1995.

On 14 May 1999, at a meeting with Muslim leaders in Syria, he was gifted and then promptly kissed a [Qur'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qur'an), an act that was controversial with some Catholics.[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177]

On 6 May 2001, he became the first Catholic pope to enter and pray in a mosque, namely the [Umayyad Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umayyad_Mosque) in [Damascus](8914.md), Syria. Respectfully removing his shoes, he entered the former [Byzantine-era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire) Christian church dedicated to [John the Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_the_Baptist), who is also revered as a [prophet of Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prophet_of_Islam). He gave a speech including the statement: "For all the times that Muslims and Christians have offended one another, we need to seek forgiveness from the Almighty and to offer each other forgiveness."[^cite_note-Mosque-108]

In 2004, John Paul II hosted the "[Papal Concert of Reconciliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_Concert_of_Reconciliation)", which brought together leaders of Islam with leaders of the Jewish community and of the Catholic Church at the Vatican for a concert by the Kraków Philharmonic Choir from Poland, the [London Philharmonic Choir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Philharmonic_Choir) from the United Kingdom, the [Pittsburgh Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_Symphony_Orchestra) from the United States, and the Ankara State Polyphonic Choir of Turkey.[^cite_note-WQED-178][^cite_note-Concert-179][^cite_note-Philharmonic-180][^cite_note-Gazette-181] The event was conceived and conducted by [Gilbert Levine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilbert_Levine), [KCSG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_St._Gregory_the_Great) and was broadcast throughout the world.[^cite_note-WQED-178][^cite_note-Concert-179][^cite_note-Philharmonic-180][^cite_note-Gazette-181]

John Paul II oversaw the publication of the [Catechism of the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_the_Catholic_Church), which makes a special provision for Muslims; therein, it is written, "together with us they adore the one, merciful God, mankind's judge on the last day."[^cite_note-Catechism-182]

#### Jainism

In 1995, John Paul II held a meeting with 21 [Jains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jains), organised by the [Pontifical Council for Interreligious Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Council_for_Interreligious_Dialogue). He praised [Mohandas Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohandas_Gandhi) for his "unshakeable faith in God", assured the Jains that the Catholic Church will continue to engage in dialogue with their religion and spoke of the common need to aid the poor. The Jain leaders were impressed with the Pope's "transparency and simplicity", and the meeting received much attention in the [Gujarat](53707.md) state in western India, home to many Jains.[^cite_note-183]

## &#x20;Assassination attempts and plots

Main articles: [Attempted assassination of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Pope_John_Paul_II), [Juan María Fernández y Krohn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mar%C3%ADa_Fern%C3%A1ndez_y_Krohn), and [Bojinka Plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bojinka_Plot)

As he entered [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square) to address an audience on 13 May 1981,[^cite_note-184] John Paul II was shot and [critically wounded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballistic_trauma) by [Mehmet Ali Ağca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_Ali_A%C4%9Fca),[^cite_note-A\&E-24][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006234-98][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDziwisz2001-185] an expert Turkish gunman who was a member of the militant fascist group [Grey Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_Wolves_\(organization\)).[^cite_note-SanFrancisco-186] The assassin used a [Browning 9 mm semi-automatic pistol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browning_Hi-Power),[^cite_note-upi2-187] shooting the Pope in the abdomen and perforating his [colon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colon_\(anatomy\)) and [small intestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_intestine) multiple times.[^cite_note-Bottum-94] John Paul II was rushed into the Vatican complex and then to the [Gemelli Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gemelli_Hospital). On the way to the hospital, he lost consciousness. Even though the two bullets missed his [superior mesenteric artery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_mesenteric_artery) and [abdominal aorta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdominal_aorta), he lost nearly three-quarters of his blood. He underwent five hours of surgery to treat his wounds.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETime_Magazine_1982-01-251-188] Surgeons performed a [colostomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colostomy), temporarily rerouting the upper part of the [large intestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_intestine) to let the damaged lower part heal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETime_Magazine_1982-01-251-188] When he briefly regained consciousness before being operated on, he instructed the doctors not to remove his [Brown Scapular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_Scapular) during the operation.[^cite_note-scapolare-189] One of the few people allowed in to see him at the Gemelli Clinic was one of his closest friends, philosopher [Anna-Teresa Tymieniecka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Teresa_Tymieniecka), who arrived on Saturday 16 May and kept him company while he recovered from emergency surgery.[^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84] The Pope later stated that the [Blessed Virgin Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary) helped keep him alive throughout his ordeal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006234-98][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDziwisz2001-185][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBertone2000–2009-190] He said:

> "Could I forget that the event in St. Peter's Square took place on the day and at the hour when the first appearance of the Mother of Christ to the poor little peasants has been remembered for over sixty years at [Fátima, Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_F%C3%A1tima)? For in everything that happened to me on that very day, I felt that extraordinary motherly protection and care, which turned out to be stronger than the deadly bullet."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPope_John_Paul_II2005184-191]

Ağca was caught and restrained by a nun and other bystanders until police arrived. He was sentenced to [life imprisonment](148681.md). Two days after Christmas in 1983, John Paul II visited Ağca in prison. John Paul II and Ağca spoke privately for about twenty minutes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaxwell-Stuart2006234-98][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDziwisz2001-185] John Paul II said, "What we talked about will have to remain a secret between him and me. I spoke to him as a brother whom I have pardoned and who has my complete trust."[^cite_note-192]

Numerous theories were advanced to explain the assassination attempt, some of them controversial. One such theory, advanced by [Michael Ledeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Ledeen) and heavily pushed by the United States [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) at the time of the assassination but never substantiated by evidence, was that the Soviet Union was behind the attempt on John Paul II's life in retaliation for the Pope's support of Solidarity, the Catholic, pro-democratic Polish workers' movement.[^cite_note-SanFrancisco-186][^cite_note-ItalianPanel-193] This theory was supported by the 2006 [Mitrokhin Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitrokhin_Commission), set up by [Silvio Berlusconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvio_Berlusconi) and headed by *[Forza Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forza_Italia_\(1994\))* senator [Paolo Guzzanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Guzzanti), which alleged that Communist [Bulgarian security departments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_for_State_Security_\(Bulgaria\)) were used to prevent the Soviet Union's role from being uncovered, and concluded that [Soviet military intelligence (*Glavnoje Razvedyvatel'noje Upravlenije*)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GRU_\(Soviet_Union\)), not the [KGB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGB), were responsible.[^cite_note-ItalianPanel-193] Russian [Foreign Intelligence Service](614404.md) spokesman Boris Labusov called the accusation "absurd".[^cite_note-ItalianPanel-193] The Pope declared during a May 2002 visit to Bulgaria that the country's Soviet-bloc-era leadership had nothing to do with the [assassination attempt](251272.md).[^cite_note-SanFrancisco-186][^cite_note-ItalianPanel-193] However, his secretary, Cardinal [Stanisław Dziwisz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Dziwisz), alleged in his book *A Life with Karol*, that the Pope was convinced privately that the former Soviet Union was behind the attack.[^cite_note-Retire2-194] It was later discovered that many of John Paul II's aides had foreign-government attachments;[^cite_note-Thomas2000-195] Bulgaria and Russia disputed the Italian commission's conclusions, pointing out that the Pope had publicly denied the Bulgarian connection.[^cite_note-ItalianPanel-193]

A second assassination attempt was made on 12 May 1982, just a day before the anniversary of the first attempt on his life, in [Fátima, Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A1tima,_Portugal), when a man tried to stab John Paul II with a [bayonet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayonet).[^cite_note-Krohn-196][^cite_note-CBC2-197][^cite_note-Reuters3-198] He was stopped by security guards. Stanisław Dziwisz later said that John Paul II had been injured during the attempt but managed to hide a non-life-threatening wound.[^cite_note-Krohn-196][^cite_note-CBC2-197][^cite_note-Reuters3-198] The assailant, a [traditionalist Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholic) Spanish priest named [Juan María Fernández y Krohn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Mar%C3%ADa_Fern%C3%A1ndez_y_Krohn),[^cite_note-Krohn-196] had been ordained as a priest by Archbishop [Marcel Lefebvre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcel_Lefebvre) of the [Society of St. Pius X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_St._Pius_X) and was opposed to the changes made by the [Second Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council), saying that the Pope was an agent of Communist Moscow and of the Marxist [Eastern Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite199595-199] Fernández y Krohn subsequently left the priesthood and served three years of a six-year sentence.[^cite_note-CBC2-197][^cite_note-Reuters3-198][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite199595-199] The ex-priest was treated for [mental illness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mental_illness) and then expelled from Portugal to become a solicitor in Belgium.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite199595-199]

The [Al-Qaeda](1921.md)-funded [Bojinka plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bojinka_plot) planned to kill John Paul II during a visit to the Philippines during World Youth Day 1995 celebrations. On 15 January 1995 a [suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_bomber) was planning to dress as a priest and detonate a bomb when the Pope passed in his [motorcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorcade) on his way to the [San Carlos Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Carlos_Seminary) in [Makati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makati). The assassination was supposed to divert attention from the next phase of the operation. However, a chemical fire inadvertently started by the cell alerted police to their whereabouts, and all were arrested a week before the Pope's visit, and confessed to the plot.[^cite_note-ThePlot-200]

In 2009 [Jack Koehler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Koehler), a journalist and former army intelligence officer, published *Spies in the Vatican: The Soviet Union's Cold War Against the Catholic Church*.[^cite_note-Koehler2011-201] Mining mostly East German and Polish secret police archives, Koehler claimed the assassination attempts were "KGB-backed".[^cite_note-202]

## Apologies

Main article: [List of apologies made by Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_apologies_made_by_Pope_John_Paul_II)

John Paul II apologised to many groups that had suffered at the hands of the Catholic Church through the years.[^cite_note-Memory-79][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPope_John_Paul_II20051-203] Before becoming pope he had been a prominent editor and supporter of initiatives such as the [Letter of Reconciliation of the Polish Bishops to the German Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letter_of_Reconciliation_of_the_Polish_Bishops_to_the_German_Bishops) from 1965. As pope, he officially made public apologies for over 100 wrongdoings, including:[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]

- The legal process on the Italian scientist and philosopher [Galileo Galilei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galileo_Galilei), himself a devout Catholic, around 1633 (31 October 1992).

  [^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208]

  [^cite_note-Adherents-209]

- The involvement of Catholics in the [Atlantic slave trade](49005.md) (9 August 1993).

- The church hierarchy's role in [burnings at the stake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_by_burning) and the [religious wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_war) that followed the [Protestant Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant_Reformation) (20 May 1995, in the Czech Republic).

- The injustices committed against women, the violation of [women's rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_rights) and the historical denigration of women (10 July 1995, in a letter to "every woman").

- The inactivity and silence of many Catholics during [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust) (see the article [Religion in Nazi Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Nazi_Germany)) (16 March 1998).

The [Great Jubilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Jubilee) of the year 2000 included a day of Prayer for Forgiveness of the Sins of the Church on 12 March 2000.

On 20 November 2001, from a laptop in the Vatican, John Paul II sent his first e-mail apologising for the [Catholic sex abuse cases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_sex_abuse_cases), the church-backed [Stolen Generations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stolen_Generations) of Aboriginal children in Australia, and to China for the behaviour of Catholic missionaries in [colonial times](7299.md).[^cite_note-PopeApologises-210]

## Health



An ailing John Paul II riding in the [Popemobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popemobile) in September 2004 in [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square)



John Paul II during a general audience in September 2004

When he became pope in 1978 at the age of 58, John Paul II was an avid sportsman who enjoyed [hiking](147313.md), [swimming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_swimming), weight training, and [skiing](28478.md), and was seen jogging in the [Vatican gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_gardens). When the cost of installing a [swimming pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_pool) in his summer residence was queried by [cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_\(Catholicism\)), the pope joked that it was "cheaper than another [conclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_conclave)".[^cite_note-211] The media contrasted his athleticism and trim figure with the poor health of [John Paul I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_I) and [Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_VI), the portliness of [John XXIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_XXIII), and the constant claims of ailments of [Pius XII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pius_XII). The only modern pope with a fitness regimen had been [Pope Pius XI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI) (1922–1939), an avid mountaineer who conquered summits including [Monte Rosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Rosa), [Mont Blanc](44768.md), and the [Matterhorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matterhorn).[^cite_note-Ratti-212][^cite_note-Ratti2-213] An *[Irish Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Independent)* article in the 1980s labelled him the *keep-fit pope*, and an Italian newspaper called him *l'atleta di Dio* ("God's athlete") in 2005.[^cite_note-214]

His health held through the 1980s, but began to unravel in the 1990s. A benign intestinal tumour was removed in 1992.[^cite_note-215] He dislocated his shoulder in a fall in 1993[^cite_note-216] and broke his femur in another fall in 1994.[^cite_note-217] In December 1995 he cut short his *[urbi et orbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbi_et_orbi)* blessing on Christmas Day after a dizzy spell.[^cite_note-218] He underwent an appendectomy in 1996.[^cite_note-219] In 2001 an orthopaedic surgeon confirmed he was suffering from [Parkinson's disease](22228064.md),[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-Parkinsons2001-221] something international observers had long suspected. The Vatican acknowledged it publicly in 2003. Despite difficulty speaking more than a few sentences at a time, trouble [hearing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hearing), and severe [osteoarthrosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osteoarthrosis), he continued to tour the world, rarely walking in public.

## Death and funeral

Main article: [Death and funeral of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_funeral_of_Pope_John_Paul_II)

### Final months

John Paul II was hospitalised with breathing problems caused by a bout of [influenza](19572217.md) on 1 February 2005.[^cite_note-222] He left the hospital on 10 February, but was subsequently hospitalised again with breathing problems two weeks later and underwent a [tracheotomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tracheotomy).[^cite_note-223]

### Final illness and death



The body of Pope John Paul II [lying in state](1505399.md)

On 31 March 2005, following a [urinary tract infection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urinary_tract_infection),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_2005-04-01-224] he developed [septic shock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septic_shock), a form of infection with a high fever and low [blood pressure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_pressure), but was not hospitalised. Instead, he was monitored by a team of consultants at his private residence. This was taken as an indication by the Pope, and those close to him, that he was nearing death; it would have been in accordance with his wishes to die in the Vatican.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_2005-04-01-224] Later that day, Vatican sources announced that John Paul II had been given the [Anointing of the Sick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anointing_of_the_Sick) by his friend and secretary Stanisław Dziwisz. The day before his death, one of his closest personal friends, [Anna-Teresa Tymieniecka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Teresa_Tymieniecka), visited him at his bedside.[^cite_note-Stourton-82][^cite_note-Bernstein/Politi-225] During the final days of the Pope's life, the lights were kept burning through the night where he lay in the Papal apartment on the top floor of the [Apostolic Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Palace). Tens of thousands of people assembled and held vigil in St. Peter's Square and the surrounding streets for two days. Upon hearing of this, the dying pope was said to have stated: "I have searched for you, and now you have come to me, and I thank you."[^cite_note-LastWords-226]

On Saturday, 2 April 2005, at approximately 15\:30 [CEST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_European_Summer_Time), John Paul II spoke his final words in [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language), *"Pozwólcie mi odejść do domu Ojca"* ("Allow me to depart to the house of the Father"), to his aides, and fell into a coma about four hours later.[^cite_note-LastWords-226][^cite_note-BBCLastWords-227] The Mass of the vigil of the Second Sunday of Easter commemorating the [canonisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canonisation) of [Faustina Kowalska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faustina_Kowalska) on 30 April 2000, had just been celebrated at his bedside, presided over by Dziwisz and two Polish associates. Present at the bedside was Cardinal [Lubomyr Husar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lubomyr_Husar) from Ukraine, who served as a priest with John Paul in Poland, along with Polish nuns of the Congregation of the [Sisters, Servants of the Most Sacred Heart of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sisters,_Servants_of_the_Most_Sacred_Heart_of_Jesus?action=edit\&redlink=1), who ran the [papal household](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefecture_of_the_Pontifical_Household). John Paul II died in his private apartment at 21\:37 CEST (19\:37 [UTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC)) of heart failure from profound [hypotension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypotension) and complete [circulatory collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circulatory_collapse) from septic shock.[^cite_note-BBCLastWords-227][^cite_note-Pisa-228][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENavarro-Valls_2_April_2005-229] His death was verified when an electrocardiogram that ran for 20 minutes showed a [flatline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatline).[^cite_note-230]

He had no close family by the time of his death; his feelings are reflected in his words written in 2000 at the end of his Last Will and Testament.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStourton2006320-231] Dziwisz later said he had not burned the pontiff's personal notes despite the request being part of the will.[^cite_note-232]

### Aftermath



(l-r) [George W. Bush](3414021.md), [Laura Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Bush), [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush), [Bill Clinton](3356.md), [Condoleezza Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condoleezza_Rice), and [Andrew Card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Card), US dignitaries paying respects to John Paul II on 6 April 2005 at [St. Peter's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Basilica), [Vatican City](32408.md)

The death of the pontiff set in motion rituals and traditions dating back to medieval times. The Rite of Visitation took place from 4 April 2005 to 7 April 2005 at St. Peter's Basilica. John Paul II's testament, published on 7 April 2005,[^cite_note-Last_Will-233] revealed that he contemplated being buried in his native Poland but left the final decision to the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals), who preferred burial beneath St. Peter's Basilica, honouring the pontiff's request to be placed "in bare earth".

The [Requiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Requiem) Mass held on 8 April 2005 was said to have set world records both for attendance and number of [heads of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heads_of_state) present at a funeral.[^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-CNN-234][^cite_note-Independent-235][^cite_note-BBCMiracle-236] *(See: [List of Dignitaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_dignitaries_at_the_funeral_of_Pope_John_Paul_II).)* It was the single largest gathering of heads of state up to that time, surpassing the funerals of [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Winston_Churchill) (1965) and [Josip Broz Tito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Josip_Broz_Tito) (1980). Four kings, five queens, at least 70 presidents and prime ministers, and more than 14 leaders of other religions attended.[^cite_note-CNN-234] An estimated four million mourners gathered in and around Vatican City.[^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-Independent-235][^cite_note-BBCMiracle-236][^cite_note-Beltway-237] Between 250,000 and 300,000 watched the event from within the Vatican's walls.[^cite_note-BBCMiracle-236] In a historical rarity, [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) and [Eastern Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox) leaders, as well as representatives and heads from Judaism, [Islam](6037917.md), [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze)[^cite_note-238] and [Buddhism](3267529.md), offered their own memorials and prayers as a way of sympathising with the grief of Catholics.

The [Dean of the College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dean_of_the_College_of_Cardinals), Cardinal [Joseph Ratzinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Ratzinger), conducted the ceremony. John Paul II was interred in the [grottoes under the basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_Grottoes), the [Tomb of the Popes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_extant_papal_tombs). He was lowered into a tomb created in the same alcove previously occupied by the remains of [John XXIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_XXIII). The alcove had been empty since John XXIII's remains had been moved into the main body of the basilica after his beatification. The remains of John Paul II were moved to the Chapel of St. Sebastian within the main basilica upon his own beatification in 2011.[^cite_note-239]

## Legacy

### Posthumous recognition

See also: [Pope John Paul II in popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II_in_popular_culture)

#### Title "the Great"



Old tomb of John Paul II in the [Vatican Grottoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_Grottoes) before his beatification

Upon the death of John Paul II, a number of clergy at the Vatican and laymen[^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-Arlington-240] began referring to the late pontiff as "John Paul the Great" –in theory only the fourth pope to be so acclaimed.[^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-Arlington-240][^cite_note-OReilly-David-241][^cite_note-Murphy-Brian-242] [Cardinal Angelo Sodano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_Angelo_Sodano) specifically referred to John Paul as "the Great" in his published written [homily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homily) for the Pope's funeral [Mass of Repose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Pope_John_Paul_II).[^cite_note-FirstSpeech-243][^cite_note-Homily-244] The South African Catholic newspaper *[The Southern Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Southern_Cross_\(South_Africa\))* has referred to him in print as "John Paul II the Great".[^cite_note-Southern-245] Some Catholic educational institutions in the US have additionally changed their names to incorporate "the Great", including [John Paul the Great Catholic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_the_Great_Catholic_University) and schools called some variant of [John Paul the Great High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_the_Great_High_School_\(disambiguation\)).



The tomb of John Paul II in the [Vatican](32408.md) Chapel of Saint Sebastian within [St. Peter's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Basilica) where it has been since 2011

Scholars of [canon law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_law_\(Catholic_Church\)) say that there is no official process for declaring a pope "Great"; the title simply establishes itself through popular and continued usage,[^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-Noonan-246][^cite_note-Noonan2-247] as was the case with celebrated secular leaders (for example, Alexander III of Macedon became popularly known as [Alexander the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_the_Great)). The three popes who today commonly are known as "Great" are [Leo I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Leo_I), who reigned from 440–461 and persuaded [Attila the Hun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attila_the_Hun) to withdraw from Rome; [Gregory I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Gregory_I), 590–604, after whom the [Gregorian chant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorian_chant) is named; and [Pope Nicholas I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Nicholas_I), 858–867, who consolidated the Catholic Church in the Western world in the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages).[^cite_note-Arlington-240]

John Paul's successor, Benedict XVI, did not use the term directly in public speeches, but made oblique references to "the great Pope John Paul II" in his first address from the [loggia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loggia) of St. Peter's Basilica, at the 20th [World Youth Day 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Youth_Day_2005) in Germany when he said in Polish: "As the great Pope John Paul II would say: Keep the flame of faith alive in your lives and your people";[^cite_note-248] and in May 2006 during a visit to Poland where he repeatedly made references to "the great John Paul" and "my great predecessor".[^cite_note-Poland2006-249]



The tomb of the parents of John Paul II at [Rakowicki Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakowicki_Cemetery) in [Kraków](16815.md), Poland
