#### Beatification

Main article: [Beatification of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatification_of_Pope_John_Paul_II)



Some 1.5 million [St. Peter's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Square) attendees witness the beatification of John Paul II on 1 May 2011 in [Vatican City](32408.md).

[^cite_note-256]

Cries of *"Santo subito! Santo subito!"* ("sainthood now!") were heard from the crowd during the pope's funeral and the conclave.[^cite_note-Moore1-257][^cite_note-Hollingshead-258][^cite_note-Hooper1-259][^cite_note-Hope-260][^cite_note-261]

Pope Benedict XVI began the beatification process for his predecessor, bypassing the normal process of waiting five years after a person's death before beginning the beatification process.[^cite_note-Hollingshead-258][^cite_note-Hooper1-259][^cite_note-Canonisation-262][^cite_note-Metro-263] In an audience with Pope Benedict XVI, [Camillo Ruini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camillo_Ruini), Vicar General of the Diocese of Rome, who was responsible for promoting the cause for canonisation of any person who died within that diocese, cited "exceptional circumstances", which suggested that the waiting period could be waived.[^cite_note-ShortBio-25][^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-Zenit3-264] This decision was announced on 13 May 2005, the Feast of [Our Lady of Fátima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_F%C3%A1tima) and the 24th anniversary of the assassination attempt on John Paul II at St. Peter's Square.[^cite_note-catholicnewsagency-265]

In early 2006, it was reported that the Vatican was investigating a possible [miracle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miracle) associated with John Paul II. [Sister Marie Simon-Pierre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_Marie_Simon-Pierre), a French nun and member of the Congregation of Little Sisters of Catholic Maternity Wards, confined to her bed by Parkinson's disease,[^cite_note-Hooper1-259][^cite_note-Vicariato-266] was reported to have experienced a "complete and lasting cure after members of her community prayed for the intercession of Pope John Paul II".[^cite_note-NYTimes-146][^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-Moore1-257][^cite_note-Hooper1-259][^cite_note-ABC-267][^cite_note-Trinity-268] As of May 2008, Sister Marie Simon-Pierre, then 46,[^cite_note-Moore1-257][^cite_note-Hooper1-259] was working again at a [maternity hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obstetrics) run by her [religious institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_institute).[^cite_note-Metro-263][^cite_note-Vicariato-266][^cite_note-Miracle-americancatholic-269][^cite_note-Willan-270]

"I was sick and now I am cured," she told reporter Gerry Shaw. "I am cured, but it is up to the church to say whether it was a miracle or not."[^cite_note-Vicariato-266][^cite_note-Miracle-americancatholic-269]

On 28 May 2006, Pope Benedict XVI celebrated Mass before an estimated 900,000 people in John Paul II's native Poland. During his [homily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homily), he encouraged prayers for the early canonisation of John Paul II and stated that he hoped canonisation would happen "in the near future".[^cite_note-Vicariato-266][^cite_note-Homily-Blonie-Park-271]



Statue of John Paul II outside the [Basilica of Our Lady of Guadalupe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Our_Lady_of_Guadalupe), Tepeyac, [Mexico City](18987.md)

In January 2007, Cardinal [Stanisław Dziwisz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Dziwisz) announced that the interview phase of the beatification process, in Italy and Poland, was nearing completion.[^cite_note-MsnbcNews2-208][^cite_note-Vicariato-266][^cite_note-Westcott-272] In February 2007, [second class relics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relics#Classification_and_prohibitions_in_the_Catholic_Church) of John Paul II—pieces of white papal [cassocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassock) he used to wear—were freely distributed with prayer cards for the cause, a typical pious practice after a saintly Catholic's death.[^cite_note-MMoore-273][^cite_note-Cause-274] On 8 March 2007, the [Vicariate of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_Vicar) announced that the diocesan phase of John Paul's cause for beatification was at an end. Following a ceremony on 2 April 2007—the second anniversary of the Pontiff's death—the cause proceeded to the scrutiny of the committee of lay, clerical, and episcopal members of the Vatican's [Congregation for the Causes of Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints), to conduct a separate investigation.[^cite_note-Hollingshead-258][^cite_note-Vicariato-266][^cite_note-Westcott-272] On the fourth anniversary of John Paul II's death, 2 April 2009, Cardinal Dziwisz, told reporters of a presumed miracle that had recently occurred at the former pope's tomb in St. Peter's Basilica.[^cite_note-Miracle-americancatholic-269][^cite_note-ncregister-275][^cite_note-Catholic-276] A nine-year-old Polish boy from [Gdańsk](12099.md), who was suffering from kidney cancer and was completely unable to walk, had been visiting the tomb with his parents. On leaving St. Peter's Basilica, the boy told them, "I want to walk," and began walking normally.[^cite_note-ncregister-275][^cite_note-Catholic-276][^cite_note-Miracle-catholicnews-277] On 16 November 2009, a panel of reviewers at the Congregation for the Causes of Saints voted unanimously that John Paul II had lived a life of heroic virtue.[^cite_note-abcNews-278][^cite_note-Catholic_Culture-279] On 19 December 2009, Pope Benedict XVI signed the first of two decrees needed for beatification and proclaimed John Paul II "Venerable", asserting that he had lived a heroic, virtuous life.[^cite_note-abcNews-278][^cite_note-Catholic_Culture-279] The second vote and the second signed decree certified the authenticity of the first miracle, the curing of Sister Marie Simon-Pierre, a French nun, from Parkinson's disease. Once the second decree is signed, the *position* (the report on the cause, with documentation about his life and writings and with information on the cause) is complete.[^cite_note-Catholic_Culture-279] He can then be beatified.[^cite_note-abcNews-278][^cite_note-Catholic_Culture-279] Some speculated that he would be beatified sometime during (or soon after) the month of the 32nd anniversary of his 1978 election, in October 2010. As Monsignor Oder said, this course would have been possible if the second decree were signed in time by Benedict XVI, stating that a posthumous miracle directly attributable to his intercession had occurred, completing the positio.



[Candles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candle) around monument to John Paul II in [Zaspa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaspa), Gdańsk, at the time of his death

The Vatican announced on 14 January 2011 that Pope Benedict XVI had confirmed the miracle involving Sister Marie Simon-Pierre and that John Paul II was to be beatified on 1 May, the Feast of Divine Mercy.[^cite_note-BBC-beatify-280] 1 May is commemorated in former Communist countries, such as Poland, and some Western European countries as [May Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Day), and John Paul II was well known for his contributions to Communism's relatively peaceful demise.[^cite_note-Bottum-94][^cite_note-CBCNews-117] In March 2011 the Polish mint issued a gold 1,000 [Polish złoty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_z%C5%82oty) coin (equivalent to US\$350), with the Pope's image to commemorate his beatification.[^cite_note-yahoo-281]

On 29 April 2011, John Paul II's coffin was disinterred from the grotto beneath St. Peter's Basilica ahead of his beatification, as tens of thousands of people arrived in Rome for one of the biggest events since his funeral.[^cite_note-Pope_John_Paul_II's_body_exhumed_ahead_of_beatification-282][^cite_note-283] John Paul II's remains, which were not exposed, were placed in front of the Basilica's main altar, where believers could pay their respect before and after the beatification Mass in St. Peter's Square on 1 May 2011. On 3 May 2011 his remains were interred in the marble altar in Pier Paolo Cristofari Chapel of [St. Sebastian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Sebastian), where [Pope Innocent XI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Innocent_XI) was buried. This more prominent location, next to the Chapel of the Pietà, the Chapel of the Blessed Sacrament, and statues of Popes Pius XI and Pius XII was intended to allow more pilgrims to view his memorial. John Paul II's body is located near the bodies of [Pope Pius X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_X) and [Pope John XXIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII), whose bodies were reinterred in the Basilica after their own beatifications and together are three of the five popes beatified in the last century. The two popes who were not exhumed and reinterred after becoming a blessed in the last century were [Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI) and [Pope John Paul I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I), who both remain entombed in the papal grottos.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]

In July 2012, a Colombian man, Marco Fidel Rojas, the former mayor of [Huila, Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huila,_Colombia), testified that he was "miraculously cured" of [Parkinson's disease](22228064.md) after a trip to Rome where he met John Paul II and prayed with him. Antonio Schlesinger Piedrahita, a renowned [neurologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurologist) in Colombia, certified Fidel's healing. The documentation was then sent to the Vatican office for sainthood causes.[^cite_note-286]

In September 2020, Poland unveiled a sculpture of him, designed by [Jerzy Kalina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerzy_Kalina?action=edit\&redlink=1) and installed outside the [National Museum, Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum,_Warsaw), holding up a [meteorite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meteorite) above a pool of red water representing blood.[^cite_note-287] In the same month, a relic containing his blood was stolen from the [Spoleto Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoleto_Cathedral) in Italy.[^cite_note-288]

#### Canonisation

Main article: [Canonization of Pope John XXIII and Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canonization_of_Pope_John_XXIII_and_Pope_John_Paul_II)



The canonisation of John Paul II and John XXIII

To be eligible for canonisation (being declared a saint) by the Catholic Church, two miracles must be attributed to a candidate.

The first miracle attributed to John Paul was the above mentioned healing of a nun's Parkinson's disease, which was recognised during the beatification process. According to an article on the Catholic News Service (CNS) dated 23 April 2013, a Vatican commission of doctors concluded that a healing had no natural (medical) explanation, which is the first requirement for a claimed miracle to be officially documented.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-Agence_France-Presse-290][^cite_note-ANSA-291]

The second miracle was deemed to have taken place shortly after the late pope's beatification on 1 May 2011; it was reported to be the healing of Costa Rican woman Floribeth Mora of an otherwise terminal [brain aneurysm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brain_aneurysm).[^cite_note-292] A Vatican panel of expert theologians examined the evidence, determined that it was directly attributable to the intercession of John Paul II, and recognised it as miraculous.[^cite_note-Agence_France-Presse-290][^cite_note-ANSA-291] The next stage was for Cardinals who compose the membership of the Congregation for the Causes of Saints to give their opinion to [Pope Francis](1687680.md) to decide whether to sign and promulgate the decree and set a date for canonisation.[^cite_note-Agence_France-Presse-290][^cite_note-ANSA-291][^cite_note-293]

On 4 July 2013, Pope Francis confirmed his approval of John Paul II's canonisation, formally recognising the second miracle attributed to his intercession. He was canonised together with John XXIII.[^cite_note-BBC_2013-20][^cite_note-294] The date of the canonisation was on 27 April 2014, Divine Mercy Sunday.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296]

The canonisation Mass for Pope John Paul II and Pope John XXIII, was celebrated by Pope Francis (with Pope Emeritus Benedict XVI), on 27 April 2014 in St. Peter's Square at the Vatican (John Paul II had died on [vigil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vigil) of Divine Mercy Sunday in 2005). About 150 cardinals and 700 bishops concelebrated the Mass, and at least 500,000 people attended the Mass, with an estimated 300,000 others watching from video screens placed around Rome.[^cite_note-297]

John Paul II's remains, considered to be holy [relics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relic), were exhumed from their place in the basilica's grotto,[^cite_note-298] and a new tomb was established at the altar of St. Sebastian.[^cite_note-299] His feast is celebrated annually on 22 October, the day of his papal inauguration, as the anniversary of his death usually falls during [Lent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lent) and often during [Holy Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Week).

#### Beatification of the Pope's parents

On 10 October 2019, the [Archdiocese of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Krak%C3%B3w) and the [Polish Episcopal Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Episcopal_Conference) approved *nihil obstat* the opening of the beatification cause of the parents of its patron saint John Paul II, Karol Wojtyła Sr. and Emilia Kaczorowska. It gained approval from the [Holy See](13393.md) to open the diocesan phase of the cause on 7 May 2020.[^cite_note-300]

### Criticism

Main article: [Criticism of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_Pope_John_Paul_II)

|  | This ["criticism" or "controversy" section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Criticism#Avoid_sections_and_articles_focusing_on_criticisms_or_controversies) **may compromise the article's [neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Neutral_point_of_view#Article_structure)**. Please help [integrate negative information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Structure) into other sections or remove [undue focus on minor criticisms and controversies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:BALASP). *(February 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

John Paul II faced some criticism for a variety of his views. He was a target of criticism from progressives for his opposition to the [ordination of women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordination_of_women), opposition to the use of [contraception](18978770.md),[^cite_note-A\&E-24][^cite_note-Condom-301] for his increased centralization of power, and for curtailing some post-Second Vatican Council practices which he deemed as going beyond what the Second Vatican Council documents authorized.[^cite_note-\:10-302]<sup>: 3 </sup> [Traditionalist Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholicism) frequently criticized him for his support for the Second Vatican Council and its reform of the [liturgy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_\(liturgy\)). John Paul II's response to [child sexual abuse within the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_sexual_abuse_within_the_Catholic_Church) has also come under heavy censure.[^cite_note-303]

#### Opus Dei controversies

Main article: [Controversies about Opus Dei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controversies_about_Opus_Dei)

John Paul II was criticised for his support of the [Opus Dei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opus_Dei) prelature and the 2002 canonisation of its founder, [Josemaría Escrivá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1), whom he called "the saint of ordinary life".[^cite_note-Opus2-304][^cite_note-Saints2-305] Other movements and religious organisations of the church went decidedly under his wing [Legion of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legion_of_Christ), the [Neocatechumenal Way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neocatechumenal_Way), [Schoenstatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schoenstatt), the [Charismatic Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charismatic_Movement), etc. And he was accused repeatedly of taking a soft hand with them, especially in the case of [Marcial Maciel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcial_Maciel), founder of the [Legionaries of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legionaries_of_Christ).[^cite_note-306]

In 1984 John Paul II appointed Joaquín Navarro-Valls, a member of Opus Dei, as Director of the [Vatican Press Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_Press_Office). An Opus Dei spokesman said that "the influence of Opus Dei in the Vatican has been exaggerated".[^cite_note-307] Of the nearly 200 cardinals in the Catholic Church, only two are known to be members of Opus Dei.[^cite_note-308]

Pope John Paul II was said to engage in [Self-flagellation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-flagellation), a practice of whipping oneself which is often used within Opus Dei.[^cite_note-BBC8375174-309][^cite_note-themonastery-310][^cite_note-311]

#### Banco Ambrosiano

Main article: [Banco Ambrosiano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banco_Ambrosiano)

John Paul II was alleged to have links with [Banco Ambrosiano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banco_Ambrosiano), an Italian bank that collapsed in 1982.[^cite_note-NYTimes-146] At the centre of the bank's failure was its chairman, [Roberto Calvi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Calvi), and his membership in the illegal [Masonic Lodge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masonic_Lodge) [Propaganda Due](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propaganda_Due) (aka P2). The [Vatican Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_Bank) was Banco Ambrosiano's main shareholder, and the death of [John Paul I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Paul_I) in 1978 is rumoured to be linked to the Ambrosiano scandal.[^cite_note-Salinger2005-147]

Calvi, often referred to as "God's Banker", was also involved with the Vatican Bank, and was close to Bishop [Paul Marcinkus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Marcinkus), the bank's chairman. Ambrosiano also provided funds for political parties in Italy, and for both the [Somoza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somoza) dictatorship in Nicaragua and its [Sandinista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandinista) opposition. It has been widely alleged that the Vatican Bank provided money for [Solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_\(Polish_trade_union\)) in Poland.[^cite_note-NYTimes-146][^cite_note-Salinger2005-147]

Calvi used his complex network of overseas banks and companies to move money out of Italy, to inflate share prices, and to arrange massive unsecured loans. In 1978, the [Bank of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Italy) produced a report on Ambrosiano that predicted future disaster.[^cite_note-Salinger2005-147] On 5 June 1982, two weeks before the collapse of Banco Ambrosiano, Calvi had written a letter of warning to John Paul II, stating that such a forthcoming event would "provoke a catastrophe of unimaginable proportions in which the Church will suffer the gravest damage".[^cite_note-TimesOnline-312] On 18 June 1982 Calvi's body was found hanging from scaffolding beneath [Blackfriars Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackfriars_Bridge) in the financial district of London. Calvi's clothing was stuffed with bricks, and contained cash valued at US\$14,000, in three different currencies.[^cite_note-GodsBanker-313]

#### Handling of sexual abuse scandals

John Paul II was criticised by representatives of the victims of clergy sexual abuse for failing to respond quickly enough to the Catholic sex abuse crisis.[^cite_note-314] After decades of inaction, the scandal came to a head when [Sinéad O'Connor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sin%C3%A9ad_O'Connor) infamously [tore up a photo of John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sin%C3%A9ad_O'Connor_on_Saturday_Night_Live) on a 3 October 1992 episode of *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live)* while performing an [a cappella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_cappella) rendition of [Bob Marley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Marley)'s "[War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_\(Bob_Marley_song\))".[^cite_note-315]

In response to mounting criticism over the next decade, John Paul II stated in 2002 that "there is no place in the priesthood and religious life for those who would harm the young".[^cite_note-Walsh-316] The Catholic Church instituted reforms to prevent future abuse by requiring [background checks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Background_check) for church employees[^cite_note-charteryouth-317] and, because a significant majority of victims were boys, disallowing ordination of men with "deep-seated homosexual tendencies".[^cite_note-vatdocord-318][^cite_note-sexabusereport-319] They now require dioceses faced with an allegation to alert the authorities, conduct an investigation and remove the accused from duty.[^cite_note-charteryouth-317][^cite_note-320] In 2008, the church asserted that the scandal was a very serious problem and estimated that it was "probably caused by 'no more than 1 per cent'", or 5,000, of the over 500,000 Catholic priests worldwide.[^cite_note-sexabuse-321][^cite_note-JohnJay-322]

In April 2002, John Paul II, despite being frail from Parkinson's disease, summoned all the American cardinals to the Vatican to discuss possible solutions to the issue of sexual abuse in the American Church. He asked them to "diligently investigate accusations". John Paul II suggested that American bishops be more open and transparent in dealing with such scandals and emphasised the role of seminary training to prevent sexual deviance among future priests. In what *[The New York Times](30680.md)* called "unusually direct language", John Paul condemned the arrogance of priests that led to the scandals:

> "Priests and candidates for the priesthood often live at a level both materially and educationally superior to that of their families and the members of their own age group. It is therefore very easy for them to succumb to the temptation of thinking of themselves as better than others. When this happens, the ideal of priestly service and self-giving dedication can fade, leaving the priest dissatisfied and disheartened."[^cite_note-323]

The Pope read a statement intended for the American cardinals, calling the sex abuse "an appalling sin" and said the priesthood had no room for such men.[^cite_note-Shame-324]

In 2002, Archbishop [Juliusz Paetz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliusz_Paetz), the Catholic Archbishop of [Poznań](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozna%C5%84), was accused of molesting seminarians.[^cite_note-325] John Paul II accepted his resignation, and placed sanctions on him, prohibiting Paetz from exercising his ministry as bishop.[^cite_note-326] It was reported that these restrictions were lifted, though Vatican spokesperson [Federico Lombardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Lombardi) strenuously denied this, saying "his rehabilitation was without foundation".

In 2003, John Paul II reiterated that "there is no place in the priesthood and religious life for those who would harm the young".[^cite_note-Walsh-316] In April 2003, a three-day conference was held, titled "Abuse of Children and Young People by Catholic Priests and Religious", where eight non-Catholic psychiatric experts were invited to speak to near all Vatican [dicasteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dicasteries)' representatives. The panel of experts overwhelmingly opposed implementation of policies of "zero-tolerance" such as was proposed by the [United States Conference of Catholic Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Conference_of_Catholic_Bishops). One expert called such policies a "case of overkill" since they do not permit flexibility to allow for differences among individual cases.[^cite_note-VaticanStudy-327]

In 2004, John Paul II recalled Cardinal [Bernard Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Francis_Law) to be Archpriest of the Papal Basilica of [Santa Maria Maggiore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Maria_Maggiore). Law had previously resigned as [Archbishop of Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Boston) in 2002 after Church documents revealed he had covered up [sexual abuse by priests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_sex_abuse_cases_in_the_United_States) in his archdiocese.[^cite_note-328] Law resigned from his Vatican position in November 2011.[^cite_note-Shame-324]

John Paul II was a firm supporter of the [Legion of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legion_of_Christ), and in 1998 discontinued investigations into [sexual misconduct by its leader Marcial Maciel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_scandal_of_Father_Marcial_Maciel), who in 2005 resigned his leadership and was later requested by the Vatican to withdraw from his ministry. However, Maciel's trial began in 2004 during the pontificate of John Paul II, but the Pope died before it ended and the conclusions were known.[^cite_note-329] (It has also been revealed that Vatican Secretary of State [Angelo Sodano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelo_Sodano) played a key role in blocking the investigations into Maciel until his removal by [Benedict XVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedict_XVI) in 2006).[^cite_note-330] In an interview with [L'Osservatore Romano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Osservatore_Romano), [Pope Francis](1687680.md) said: "I am grateful to Pope Benedict, who dared to say this publicly (when more facts began to come to light after Degollado's death in 2008, Pope Benedict XVI in 2010 launched another investigation and on 1 May 2010 announced a declaration about the crimes of the founder of the Legionaries), and to Pope John Paul II, who dared to give the green light to the Legionaries' case".[^cite_note-331]

On 10 November 2020, the Vatican published a report which found that John Paul II learned of allegations of sexual impropriety against former cardinal [Theodore McCarrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_McCarrick)—at the time the Archbishop of Newark—in 1999. The pope was informed via a letter from Cardinal [John O'Connor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_O'Connor_\(cardinal\)) of New York, warning him that appointing McCarrick as [Archbishop of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Washington) would be a mistake. John Paul II ordered an investigation, which stalled when three of the four bishops tasked with investigating claims allegedly brought back "inaccurate or incomplete information". John Paul II planned on not giving McCarrick the appointment anyway, but relented after McCarrick wrote a letter of denial. He created McCarrick a [cardinal](6221.md) in 2001. McCarrick would eventually be laicized after it was shown that he abused minors.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] [George Weigel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Weigel), a biographer of John Paul II, defended the Pope's actions, saying "McCarrick fooled a lot of people ... and he deceived John Paul II in a way that is laid out in almost biblical fashion in \[the Vatican's] report".[^cite_note-334]

In a 2019 interview with Mexican television, Pope Francis defended John Paul II's legacy on protecting minors against clerical sexual abuse. He said that John Paul II was "often misled", as in the case of [Hans Hermann Groër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Hermann_Gro%C3%ABr). Francis said that with respect to the case of [Marcial Maciel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcial_Maciel):

> "Ratzinger was courageous, and so was John Paul II. ... With respect to John Paul II, we have to understand certain attitudes because he came from a closed world, from behind the [Iron Curtain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Curtain), where communism was still in force. There was a defensive mentality. We have to understand this well, and no one can doubt the saintliness of this great man and his good will. He was great, he was great."[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336]

On 6 March 2023, an investigative report by the Polish television station [TVN24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVN24) concluded that "there \[is now] no doubt" that John Paul II "knew about [sexual abuse of children by priests](158934.md) under his authority and sought to conceal it when he was an archbishop in his native Poland". The Dutch journalist [Ekke Overbeek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekke_Overbeek) released a book on John Paul II with similar claims the following week.

In response to the claims, Pope Francis stated: "You have to put things in the context of the era\[...] At that time everything was covered up. \[...] It was only when the [Boston scandal broke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_abuse_scandal_in_the_Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Boston) that the church began to look at the problem." The [Polish Episcopal Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Episcopal_Conference) stated that "'further archival research' would be needed to arrive at a just evaluation of the decisions and actions" of John Paul II.[^cite_note-337] Furthermore, other journalists have criticised the report, including the sources and their interpretation. One of the sources used in the report was the controversial American archbishop [Rembert Weakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rembert_Weakland), an opponent of the pontiff who himself committed sexual misconduct and covered up clerical abuse.[^cite_note-\:1-338][^cite_note-\:2-339] Another point of contention is the use of materials from the [communist secret police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Public_Security_\(Poland\)) in the report.[^cite_note-Skibinski-340]

Archives of the [Archdiocese of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Krak%C3%B3w) were opened on 10 February 2026.[^cite_note-341] Journalists from [*Rzeczpospolita*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rzeczpospolita_\(newspaper\)) conducted an analysis of the archival material pertaining to John Paul II's time as Archbishop of Kraków and concluded that there was no evidence that he covered up cases of abuse, and that some of his actions in the matter were above the standards of the time.[^cite_note-342]

#### Problems with traditionalists

John Paul II was criticised by some [traditionalist Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholics), in addition to those demanding modernisation. Points of contention with traditionalists included demanding a return to the [Tridentine Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tridentine_Mass),[^cite_note-HughHewitt-343] as well as the repudiation of reforms instituted after the Second Vatican Council, such as the use of the vernacular language in the formerly Latin-language [Roman Rite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Rite), [ecumenism](146656.md), and the principle of [religious liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_liberty).[^cite_note-344] In 1988, the controversial traditionalist Archbishop [Marcel Lefebvre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcel_Lefebvre), founder of the [Society of Saint Pius X](232299.md) (1970), was [excommunicated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excommunicated) under John Paul II because of [the unapproved ordination of four bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_%C3%89c%C3%B4ne_consecrations), which Cardinal Ratzinger called a "schismatic act".[^cite_note-345]

The [World Day of Prayer for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Day_of_Prayer_for_Peace),[^cite_note-Assisi4-346] a 1986 meeting in Assisi, Italy in which the pope prayed only with Christians,[^cite_note-SanRufino-347] was criticised for giving the impression that [syncretism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncretism) and [indifferentism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indifferentism) were openly embraced by the Papal [Magisterium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magisterium). When a second Day of Prayer for Peace in the World was held in 2002,[^cite_note-DayOfPrayer-348] it was condemned as confusing the laity and compromising to false religions. Likewise criticised was his kissing of the Qur'an in Damascus, Syria, on one of his travels on 6 May 2001.[^cite_note-Qu'ran-349] His call for religious freedom was not always supported; bishops like [Antônio de Castro Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B4nio_de_Castro_Mayer) promoted [religious tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_tolerance) but at the same time rejected the Vatican II principle of religious liberty as being liberalist and already condemned by [Pope Pius IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX) in his *[Syllabus errorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syllabus_errorum)* (1864) and at the [First Vatican Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Vatican_Council).[^cite_note-350]

#### Religion and AIDS

See also: [Catholic Church and HIV/AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_and_HIV/AIDS)

John Paul II continued the tradition of advocating for the [culture of life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_life). In solidarity with [Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI)'s *[Humanae vitae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanae_vitae)*, he rejected artificial birth control, even in the use of condoms to prevent the spread of [AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIDS).[^cite_note-Condom-301] Critics have said that large families are caused by lack of contraception and exacerbate [Third World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_World) poverty and problems, such as street children in South America. John Paul II argued that the proper way to prevent the spread of AIDS was not condoms but rather "correct practice of sexuality, which presupposes chastity and fidelity".[^cite_note-Condom-301]

#### Social programmes

There was strong criticism of the Pope for the controversy surrounding the alleged use of charitable social programmes as a means of converting people in the Third World to Catholicism.[^cite_note-AsiaNews2-351][^cite_note-IndiaStar-352] The Pope created an uproar in the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent) when he suggested that a great harvest of faith would be witnessed on the subcontinent in the third Christian millennium.[^cite_note-NCR2-353]

#### Argentine military regime

John Paul II endorsed Cardinal [Pio Laghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pio_Laghi), who critics say supported the [Dirty War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirty_War) in Argentina and was on friendly terms with the Argentine generals of the [military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Reorganization_Process), playing regular tennis matches with the Navy's representative in the junta, Admiral [Emilio Eduardo Massera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_Eduardo_Massera).[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357]

#### Ian Paisley

In 1988, when John Paul II was delivering a speech to the [European Parliament](9581.md), [Ian Paisley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ian_Paisley), the leader of the [Democratic Unionist Party](221428.md) and [Moderator of the General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderator_of_the_General_Assembly) of the [Free Presbyterian Church of Ulster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Presbyterian_Church_of_Ulster), shouted "I denounce you as the [Antichrist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antichrist)!"[^cite_note-Paisley_makes_point-358][^cite_note-359] He held up a red banner reading "Pope John Paul II ANTICHRIST". [Otto von Habsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otto_von_Habsburg) (the last [Crown Prince of Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Prince_of_Austria-Hungary)), a [Member of the European Parliament](258909.md) (MEP) for Germany, snatched Paisley's banner, tore it up, and along with other MEPs helped eject him from the chamber.[^cite_note-Paisley_makes_point-358][^cite_note-BBCIreland-360][^cite_note-Paisley-361][^cite_note-NYT5-362][^cite_note-Cloud-363] The Pope continued with his address after Paisley had been ejected.[^cite_note-BBCIreland-360][^cite_note-MacDonald-364][^cite_note-Angelique-365]

#### Međugorje apparitions

See also: [Our Lady of Međugorje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Me%C4%91ugorje)

A number of quotes about the [apparitions of Međugorje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_response_to_the_Medjugorje_apparitions), in [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md), have been attributed to John Paul II.[^cite_note-366] In 1998, when a certain German gathered various statements that were supposedly made by the Pope and Cardinal Ratzinger, and then forwarded them to the Vatican in the form of a memorandum, Ratzinger responded in writing on 22 July 1998: "The only thing I can say regarding statements on [Međugorje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me%C4%91ugorje) ascribed to the Holy Father and myself is that they are \[*frei erfunden*] complete invention".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKutleša2001283-367] Similar claims were also rebuked by the Vatican's Secretariate of State.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKutleša2001256-368]

#### Beatification controversy

Some Catholic theologians disagreed with the call for the beatification of John Paul II. Eleven dissident theologians, including [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit) professor José María Castillo and Italian theologian [Giovanni Franzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Franzoni), said that his stance against contraception and the ordination of women as well as the church scandals during his pontificate presented "facts which according to their consciences and convictions should be an obstacle to beatification".[^cite_note-369] Some traditionalist Catholics opposed his beatification and canonisation for his views on liturgy and participation in prayer with enemies of the church, heretics and non-Christians.[^cite_note-370]

After the 2020 report about the handling of the sexual misconduct complaints against [Theodore McCarrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_McCarrick), some called for John Paul II's sainthood to be revoked.[^cite_note-371]

## Personal life



[Anna-Teresa Tymieniecka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Teresa_Tymieniecka) maintained a thirty-year friendship with Pope John Paul II.

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon [Presentation by Carl Bernstein on *His Holiness: John Paul II and the Hidden History of Our Time*, September 24, 1996](https://www.c-span.org/video/?75377-1/pope-john-paul-ii), [C-SPAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-SPAN) |

Wojtyła was a [Cracovia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KS_Cracovia) football team supporter, and the club retired number 1 in his honour.[^cite_note-372] Having played the game himself as a goalkeeper, Wojtyla was a fan of English football team [Liverpool F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.), where his compatriot [Jerzy Dudek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerzy_Dudek) played in the same position.[^cite_note-373]

In 1973, while still the archbishop of Kraków, Wojtyła befriended a Polish-born, later American philosopher, [Anna-Teresa Tymieniecka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Teresa_Tymieniecka). The thirty-two-year friendship (and occasional academic collaboration) lasted until his death.[^cite_note-Stourton-82][^cite_note-Guardian-letters-83][^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84] She served as his host when he visited New England in 1976, and photos show them together on skiing and camping trips.[^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84] Letters that he wrote to her were part of a collection of documents sold by Tymieniecka's estate in 2008 to the [National Library of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Library_of_Poland).[^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84] According to the [BBC](19344654.md) the library had initially kept the letters from public view, partly because of John Paul's path to sainthood, but a library official announced in February 2016 the letters would be made public.[^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84][^cite_note-374] In February 2016, the BBC documentary program *[Panorama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panorama_\(TV_series\))* reported that John Paul II had apparently had a close relationship with the Polish-born philosopher.[^cite_note-Telegraph_2016-84][^cite_note-Panarama-85] The pair exchanged personal letters over 30 years, and Stourton believes that Tymieniecka had confessed her love for Wojtyła.[^cite_note-Stourton-82][^cite_note-375] The Vatican described the documentary as "more smoke than fire", and Tymieniecka denied being involved with John Paul II.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377]

Writers [Carl Bernstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Bernstein), the veteran investigative journalist of the [Watergate scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watergate_scandal), and Vatican expert [Marco Politi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Politi), were the first journalists to talk to Anna-Teresa Tymieniecka in the 1990s about her importance in John Paul's life. They interviewed her and dedicated 20 pages to her in their 1996 book *His Holiness*.[^cite_note-Stourton-82][^cite_note-Bernstein/Politi-225][^cite_note-378] Bernstein and Politi even asked her if she had ever developed any romantic relationship with John Paul II, "however one-sided it might have been". She responded, "No, I never fell in love with the cardinal. How could I fall in love with a middle-aged clergyman? Besides, I'm a married woman."[^cite_note-Stourton-82][^cite_note-Bernstein/Politi-225]

Dear brothers and sisters, after the great Pope John Paul II, the cardinals have elected me, a simple and humble worker in the Lord's vineyard. The fact that the Lord can work and act even with insufficient means consoles me, and above all I entrust myself to your prayers. In the joy of the resurrected Lord, we go on with his help. He is going to help us and Mary will be on our side. Thank you.
